Bundesliga Player Ratings | Freiburg 2 -0 Union Berlin

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Julian Schuster’s SC Freiburg have responded to Bundesliga matchday nine victories from Bayern Munich, Borussia Dortmund, and RB Leipzig with one of their own. Schuster’s Breisgauer secured their sixth Bundesliga victory of the 2026/27 campaign, consolidating their top-four place as European football heads into the final international break of the 2026 calendar year. Freiburg will spend the next weeks in third place in the Bundesliga table after defeating Union Berlin 2-0 at home.

Schuster made four changes to the team he fielded in Thursday’s Conference League encounter in Trabzon. Recently recalled German national team right back Philipp Treu was deemed fit enough to return to the starting XI, moving Thursday’s goal-scorer Lukas Kübler back to the bench. In attack, Yuito Suzuki, Jan-Niklas Beste, and Treu’s fellow German national team recall Derry Scherhant took over for Cyriaqué Irié, Keisuke Goto, and Vincenzo Grifo. Lustrinelli, by contrast, stuck with the XI that earned his team their third consecutive league draw against Köln last weekend.

The fact that the visitors from Köpenick possessed a full week of rest was certainly apparent in the opening minutes. Union pressed hard off the ball and managed to disrupt the favored side’s rhythm over the course of the opening ten minutes. Freiburg found themselves prone to turnovers, plenty of misplaced passes, and blocked shots as they attempted to drive toward the FCU net. Igor Matanovic placed a header on target in the 9th minute, a relatively soft effort that at least constituted the Breisgau club’s first attempt on goal.

Impressively enough, Lustrinelli’s charges continued to stand their ground as the quarter-of-an-hour mark passed. The hosts did begin looking more comfortable in possession, but Union ensured that Freiburg at no point looked truly dangerous. Scherhant worked his way into the box on a couple of occasions, and there were more crosses sent in Matanovic’s direction. Freiburg nevertheless remained without a genuine scoring chance. Even off a free kick in a promising position (19th), a potentially dangerous long throw (20th), and a full scramble in front of the net (24th), Schuster’s men simply couldn’t threaten.

Despite trying what appeared to be every last possible permutation on attack builds, Schuster’s men simply couldn’t achieve the breakthrough. A match that, admittedly, featured some sloppy play from both teams descended into a prolonged lull as halftime approached. In many cases, SCF passing sequences involving ten or more passes simply weren’t enough to draw Lustrinelli’s team out of their strict zonal lock. SCF players rotated, dropped deep, and passed themselves dizzy to no avail.

Apart from a Suzuki effort in the 37th minute and another Matanovic header in the 42nd, nothing came close to troubling FCU keeper Frederik Rønnow. Both chances did result in corners, from which the Schwarzwälder simply couldn’t produce anything. Halftime arrived with every last match statistic ridiculously skewed in the direction of the hosts. Metrics such as a literal 74-25 possession split and 10-0 shot tally nevertheless didn’t translate to anything beyond a 0-0 scoreline.

A double substitution at the break at least served as an attempt by the Union trainer to liven up proceedings somewhat. Two new offensive actors, Woo-Yeong Jeong and the notoriously pacy Scotsman Oliver Burke, entered in order to at least give Union a chance to engineer a few chances on the counter. There were one or two instances in which Rønnow came close to unlocking Burke on quick counters, but Union weren’t able to accomplish much beyond opening themselves up slightly at the back.

Freiburg managed to threaten in a more consistent fashion than during the opening 45, with Scherhant (51st) and Suzuki (52nd) knocking on the door. Suzuki came very close on his chance, lacing the ball into the side netting from close range. Suzuki would initially be credited with the ball that did find the back of the net in the 55th minute. Later, however, replays confirmed that Beste had last touched the ball that ended up being diverted by FCU captain Rani Khedira into his own net. Freiburg were finally up 1-0 ten minutes after the restart.

Union naturally began to take more risks after the opener and were eventually undone by a Freiburg counter some 12 minutes later. Union broke through the SCF lines on a pair of occasions, but couldn’t generate any real scoring chances. Burke found himself in the best position to draw the capital city side level in the 61st, yet could only shoot over. German international Scherhant typically sprung the SCF counters back the other way. It was Scherhant who quickly sent Suzuki into space after a high recovery for the 2-0 in the 67th minute. The nine-cap Japanese international finished coolly over the onrushing Rønnow.

Union posed no significant challenge after Suzuki doubled the advantage. In point of fact, die Eisernen could count themselves exceedingly lucky that Freiburg didn’t run up the score. Vincenzo Grifo failed to convert a penalty in the 84th minute. Off the ensuing corner after the penalty, freshly subbed-on SCF striker Lucas Höler found himself denied the 3-0 by an excellent Rønnow reflex save. The final three minutes of normal time and five minutes of added time were played in silence whilst both sets of supporters acknowledged a medical emergency in the stands.

Mio Backhaus (6) – Philipp Treu (6), Matthias Ginter (6), Philipp Lienhart (6), Jordy Makengo (6) – Maximilian Eggestein (5), Yannik Engelhardt (7), Jan-Niklas Beste (8), Yuito Suzuki (9), Derry Scherhant (6) – Igor Matanovic (5)

Substitutes: Keisuke Goto (5), Vincenzo Grifo (5), Patrick Osterhage (5), Lukas Kübler (-), Lucas Höler (-)

Frederik Rönnow (6) – Janik Haberer (6), Zeno van den Bosch (5), Marvin Friedrich (4), Derrick Köhn (3) – Rani Khedira (4), Andras Schäfer (5), Michel Aebischer (4), Kastriot Imeri (4), Livan Burcu (4) – Tim Skarke (3)

Substitutes: Woo-Yeong Jeong (4), Oliver Burke (4), Andrej Ilic (3), Linus Güther (4), Tom Rothe (3)

One of Freiburg’s most determined players throughout was No. 10 Yuito Suzuki. The player previously profiled by Get German Football News had appeared to lose some of his resolve this season and, indeed, the 25-year-old hadn’t managed to get his name on a match scoresheet since his unfortunate sending-off against Schalke on matchday five. Some talk of Suzuki being replaced by countryman Keisuke Goto had cropped up recently. Suzuki’s performance from the very start appeared to be something of a response to this.

The Japanese international certainly backed up his determination with the necessary physical effort. Suzuki covered 10.53 kilometres, registered 25 sprints, and reached a top speed of 32.9 km/h. His tireless running helped him remain involved throughout, while his willingness to attack spaces and take on opponents provided Freiburg with a valuable attacking outlet.

Suzuki recorded 44 touches, completed 77 percent of his 30 passes, and succeeded with one of his two attempted dribbles. The 25-year-old also created one shooting opportunity and contested seven duels, winning three.

Most importantly, Suzuki finally made his mark on the scoresheet again. His goal came from one of three shots and an accumulated expected goals tally of 0.50. The finish supplied the tangible reward for a performance characterized by determination, movement, and renewed attacking confidence.

After a frustrating period in which his place in the starting XI had increasingly come under discussion, Suzuki delivered precisely the sort of response Freiburg needed from their Japanese playmaker.

Jan-Niklas Beste deserves special recognition for an extraordinarily industrious performance on Freiburg’s flank. The 27-year-old absolutely ran his socks off over the full 90 minutes, covering 11.56 kilometres, completing 32 sprints, and reaching a top speed of 32.5 km/h. Beste’s tireless running and willingness to carry the ball forward helped Freiburg cover considerable ground in attack.

The German international’s contributions were by no means restricted to physical effort. Beste registered an assist, created four shooting opportunities, and accumulated 0.23 expected assists. He also recorded 69 touches, attempted six dribbles with a 67 percent success rate, and completed half of his four crosses.

Beste’s commitment extended to his defensive and physical work as well. He won 60 percent of his ten contested duels, repeatedly demonstrating the determination required to compete across the entire pitch.

Suzuki may have earned the top individual honour, but Beste’s exceptional work rate, attacking enterprise, and direct contribution to the scoresheet were equally important elements of Freiburg’s performance.

Skyrim lead designer Bruce Nesmith loved that Baldur's Gate 3 closed off story paths based on your decisions, but defends Bethesda's approach: 'They were both fun, they're just fun in different ways'

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

I love being Cyrodiil's one-man deep state in charge of all major economic, social, criminal, and paramilitary organizations.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Speaking on a new episode of The Examined Game, Skyrim lead designer Bruce Nesmith shared his big takeaway from Baldur's Gate 3, and contrasted the approaches Bethesda and Larian have taken in their respective RPGs.

"The surprises come with the little things, not the big things. I have a long history with D&D as well as Bethesda. For me, it was joyful to see the D&D expression of fantasy rules come to life. Larian Studios did a magnificent job of that. I also thought that they redefined things in a certain way. Bethesda games have always had the philosophy of, let the player play everything.

"You can play the entire Thieves' Guild, you can play the entire Fighters' Guild, you can play the entire Mages' Guild, etc. There is nothing that your character can't play. That's a very good philosophy. I support that philosophy. But I was pleasantly surprised to see how positive it felt to me to make consequential decisions that closed off gameplay in Baldur's Gate.

"That also felt good, because it was done well. I felt like the decisions I made had more meaning in certain ways, because there were actual consequences. If I wanted to see those other [outcomes], I'd have to bring up another character and try different paths.

"They were both fun, they're just fun in different ways, and that, to me, was part of the surprise. I would have expected to fall on one side or the other, as if they were things [where] you couldn't have both. The lesson learned is: You can have both. I can enjoy this game over here that does it this way, and enjoy this game over here that does it that way."

I think Nesmith is 100% correct, and it's really Bethesda's lane to offer relatively unhindered fantasy fulfillment in its games. You generally see players and modders be the ones to introduce roleplaying-oriented restrictions, while Bethesda maximizes freedom.

At the same time, I've always thought that what separates Bethesda games from more pure sim sandboxes like Mount & Blade is that the studio provides the player with just enough structure and narrative to push back on. I think there's room to experiment there, and examples in Bethesda's own back catalogue of where it can take things.

Morrowind springs to mind: The Fighters' and Thieves' Guilds are opposed to each other, and their quests are mutually exclusive after a point⁠—you even directly sabotage the other guild in each questline. The Mages' Guild and House Telvanni are similarly opposed, and the Dunmer Houses pretty much all hate each other.

But you don't even have to go all the way back to 2002: Skyrim has three major examples in the Civil War questline, the Dawnguard expansion, and the alternate ending to the Dark Brotherhood story. Siding with the Penitus Oculatus in "Destroy the Dark Brotherhood" isn't as substantial as the faction quest you're throwing away, but it is a very cool addition that enhances Skyrim's sense of depth and stakes. I'd love to see more like it in TES6: Sentinel⁠—wouldn't it be rad to have a full questline for both the secret police and the assassins' guild?

I caught on Nesmith's choice of words that he enjoyed Baldur's Gate 3's approach "because it was done well." I've certainly encountered content-blocking choices in games that felt cheap or forced. I'm a huge CD Projekt fan, but I'll always be a hater when it comes to The Witcher 2's mutually exclusive second acts. I think the same idea was executed much better in Cyberpunk 2077: Phantom Liberty.

Flare CEO Pushes Back Against XRP 'FUD'

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Flare CEO Hugo Philion has expressed his frustration about recent fear, uncertainty, and doubt (FUD) around XRP and reiterated that the future of the cryptocurrency should not be centered solely around Ripple.

The Flare CEO recently addressed in an X post the constant habit of XRP holders to evaluate the future of the cryptocurrency through the prism of what Ripple is doing and what it isn't.

"The value and narrative of XRP should never have been viewed as being dependent on the actions of a single actor," the Flare CEO said.

The Flare CEO emphasized that XRP holders should instead concentrate their efforts on finding ways to improve the usability of the cryptocurrency.

In this context, Philion said that XRP holders need to consider three main questions.

The first question is what XRP holders are actually doing to utilize their XRP holdings. The second is how they can convince potential investors to hold XRP.

Finally, the third point that the Flare CEO mentioned is that the XRP community should find a way to transform XRP from a predominantly speculative asset into usable capital.

"And that's part of the reason we created Flare because we feel like there's always been more that can and should be done with XRP," the Flare CEO added.

Besides, the Flare CEO also invited XRP holders to try Flare. According to Philion, it's a way to make XRP less dependent on Ripple.

The Flare CEO's statements come amid increasing frustration around XRP and the lackluster performance of the cryptocurrency.

Over the past year, the price of XRP has gone down by around 50%, and at press time, the price of the cryptocurrency stands at $1.16.

Recently, Ripple's former chief technology officer David Schwartz also faced some criticism over the poor performance of XRP. Schwartz noted that his influence on the XRP price was very limited.

One X user raised a question about whether FLR holders are doing enough to demonstrate the value of the cryptocurrency, especially when it's possible for XRP holders to use Flare's decentralized finance ecosystem without purchasing FLR.

Still, Philion dismissed the claim that there is no need for FLR when using FXRP (a tokenized version of XRP on Flare).

"It's incorrect btw that you can use FXRP without needing FLR," the Flare CEO clarified.

According to Philion, what Flare Smart Accounts do is simply abstract away the need to manage FLR and make the experience more convenient. At the same time, FLR is used to pay transaction fees on the underlying networks.

Besides, the Flare CEO also underlined that in the long term, there should be more use cases for the token apart from transaction fees.

"Usage is important. Need to get fee generation going in a broader way in the ecosystem," he added.

Web3Auth says it frontran hackers by moving $5.6 million out of exposed Keplr wallets

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Consensys' Web3Auth team said it used a flaw that exposed some Keplr Wallet users' private keys to move their funds to safe addresses. Its 12 recovery wallets currently hold about $5.6 million.

Web3Auth, the Consensys-owned service that runs Google and Apple sign-in for crypto wallet Keplr, said it moved funds out of vulnerable Keplr wallets to keep them away from an attacker. The 12 addresses it says hold the recovered funds had about $5.6 million in crypto as of publicatino time on Sunday, according to onchain data reviewed by The Block.

A flaw in Web3Auth's systems exposed the private keys of some Keplr Wallet users who signed up with a Google or Apple account, the firms said. Though it was initially designed for the Cosmos ecosystem, Keplr now supports now supports many non-Cosmos chains, including EVM networks, and said in 2025 that it had more than 2 million users.

"This was not a hack of Keplr's code. The incident originated at Web3Auth, a provider Keplr uses," Keplr co-founder Josh Lee wrote on X, estimating that about 20% of Keplr's Google and Apple login accounts were affected. Users who set up Keplr with a recovery phrase or a hardware wallet are not affected, Keplr said.

Web3Auth said on Saturday it had started emptying vulnerable wallets the attacker hadn't reached yet, using "the same path of the original attacker." "Funds recovered through this process are safe, and we are working with Keplr on a path to recovery for users," Web3Auth said.

Web3Auth's reference to an "original attacker" suggests some wallets were drained before the recovery began. Web3Auth has not said how many wallets the attacker hit or how much was taken, and did not immediately respond to a request for further information from The Block.

"We are aware that some of these transactions have been mistaken for attacker activity," Web3Auth said early Sunday, when it published its recovery addresses. Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery had reported on Saturday that a thief collected about $6 million from more than 44,000 Keplr wallets. All eight addresses Bitquery tied to the thief are on Web3Auth's recovery list.

Most of the recovered funds are on Cosmos Hub, where Web3Auth's recovery address holds about 2.49 million ATOM Cosmos Hub (ATOM), worth roughly $3.3 million. Much of that ATOM had been staked, and the sweeps converted it into tokens that could be moved right away instead of waiting out the network's 21-day withdrawal period.

Lee said on Saturday that many vulnerable accounts may have dodged the attack. "It appears only a fraction of that 20% has actually been exploited, and the remaining accounts may be recoverable," he wrote. Keplr warned that some accounts "may already show a reduced or empty balance as a result of these mitigation efforts."

Keplr told Google and Apple login users not to send any funds to their accounts, advising them to move any remaining funds to a wallet secured by a recovery phrase or a hardware device. Keplr said it is working with Web3Auth on a way for users to check whether their accounts were affected.

Web3Auth first disclosed the issue on Friday afternoon and paused the service that lets apps create wallets through social logins. It said it had found no evidence that users of MetaMask, Consensys' flagship wallet, were affected. Consensys acquired Web3Auth in June 2025 and now markets the service as MetaMask Embedded Wallets, according to its developer documentation.

The Block has reached out to Web3Auth, Consensys and Keplr for comment.

The diesel problem that could spoil Bitcoin’s hopes for cheaper money

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

National inventories fell to 99.26 million barrels, while lower weekly diesel and heating-oil prices kept the financing risk conditional.

U.S. inventories of distillate fuel oil, which includes diesel and heating oil, fell below 100 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 30, even as the latest surveyed fuel prices declined. The smaller stock buffer leaves fuel costs a potential obstacle to inflation relief and easier financing for Bitcoin investors.

The U.S. Energy Information Administration's Nov. 4 report showed stocks of 99.26 million barrels, down from 102.82 million a week earlier. That 3.57-million-barrel decline left inventories 13% below their five-year average.

U.S. on-highway diesel averaged $6.29 a gallon on Nov. 2, down 6 cents in a week. EIA's surveyed residential heating-oil average fell 9.2 cents to $6.14 a gallon over the same period. Diesel prices include taxes; the heating-oil series excludes them.

Both fuels remained substantially more expensive than a year earlier: diesel was up $2.54 a gallon and residential heating oil was up $2.51.

There is also continuing refinery supply. Utilization increased to 94.5% from 92.8% in the week ended Oct. 30, although distillate production edged down to 5.154 million barrels a day from 5.161 million. Higher overall refinery activity therefore coexisted with a further draw on distillate stocks.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

National distillate stocks fell below 100 million barrels. The latest surveyed diesel and heating-oil prices fell weekly, while staying higher than a year earlier.

Nov. 2, 2026: both surveyed fuel prices fell from the preceding week. Their small declines sit near zero on the common scale.

Nov. 2, 2026: the same prices remained much higher than a year earlier. Weekly relief does not erase elevated fuel costs.

A smaller stock buffer can coexist with falling weekly prices; Bitcoin's financing risk remains conditional.

National stocks and surveyed fuel prices have different observation dates. Diesel includes taxes; residential heating oil excludes them. The 100-million mark is not an exhaustion boundary. Neither view measures inflation pass-through, Fed repricing or a Bitcoin reaction.

Stock positions use a labelled linear 95–105 million-barrel scale. Fuel-price change positions use a common linear -$3 to +$3 per-gallon scale in both views. Prices and changes are EIA reported figures; no inflation or Bitcoin response is calculated.

National stocks and surveyed fuel prices have different observation dates. Diesel includes taxes; residential heating oil excludes them. The 100-million mark is not an exhaustion boundary. Neither view measures inflation pass-through, Fed repricing or a Bitcoin reaction.

Diesel powers freight, while heating oil adds directly to household bills. Persistent fuel costs can make moving goods and heating homes more expensive. If that pressure slows broader inflation relief, it could obstruct easier financing conditions for Bitcoin investors.

Historical research explains why that possibility matters. Authors of the August 2023 International Monetary Fund working paper The Crypto Cycle and US Monetary Policy found that Fed tightening reduced a common crypto-price measure through investors' willingness to take risk.

The Fed held its target range at 3.75% to 4% on Oct. 28 and said inflation remained elevated. That decision preceded the inventory release.

EIA's next weekly petroleum report is scheduled for Nov. 12 at noon Eastern, covering the week ended Nov. 6. Sustained inventory rebuilding alongside lower fuel costs would weaken the inflation concern. Continued draws would keep attention on the stock buffer, but their significance for Bitcoin would depend on whether fuel pressure reaches broader inflation and financing conditions.

Resident Evil Requiem Feedback Resulted In Veronica And DLC Delay

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

A reliable Resident Evil insider has claimed that the main reason for Veronica’s reported delay is because of the response to Requiem and some of the common complaints it faced.

It’s easy to forget considering how absolutely stacked 2026 has been, but it’s not even been a year since Resident Evil Requiem launched and gave us the series’ biggest game to date. Despite that, Capcom clearly doesn’t plan on slowing down, as it followed up Leon's return with the reveal of the long-rumoured Code Veronica remake.

Resident Evil Veronica’s showstopping Summer Game Fest reveal is coming up on being half a year ago, and we’ve heard literally nothing about it since then. A reliable Resident Evil insider recently claimed this is because of a delay that’s pushed the game into Fall 2027, and they’ve now shared why that was done.

Earlier today, AestheticGamer1 responded to a post on Twitter complaining about The Game Awards and Summer Game Fest getting all of the Resident Evil reveals. In response, Dusk explained that the lack of marketing for Veronica and the Requiem DLC isn’t tied to that, and that it’s actually due to the game’s development.

Capcom delivers its most nuanced combination of action and horror yet with Resident Evil Requiem.

According to them, Capcom was in a “busy crunch period” earlier in the year to try and get Veronica out in the first quarter of 2027, which slipped back to Q2 and eventually to Fall. AestheticGamer1 says that Capcom could have released Veronica in early 2027 but, “especially after Requiem feedback”, it wants to give both it and the still unseen story DLC more time.

Another trailer for Veronica was apparently planned to have been released by now, but was pushed back alongside the game.

I have to imagine that last bit is talking about the back end of Leon’s campaign, which many think is the low point of Requiem.

As frustrating as it’s been to go so long without any kind of update on the future of Resident Evil, it’s definitely better that Capcom is listening to everyone about what needs to be improved and giving itself the time to do so. And hey, if any series has earned itself a bit of a break, it’s Resident Evil.

He played for West Brom & Bolton Wanderers - Then he was jailed over match-fixing plot

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

For 16 years, Delroy Facey was a familiar face in the EFL, but shortly after his playing career came to an end, shocking revelations were made about him which ended up with the former player in prison.

Over the course of a career that lasted for almost two decades, Delroy Facey made over 450 appearances in the senior game and was largely considered a stalwart player of the EFL's three divisions. He also represented Grenada twelve times at an international level, and even made ten Premier League appearances for Bolton Wanderers.

But shortly after his career ended, it became clear that he had been involved in a match-fixing scandal which tarnished his entire legacy as a player, and which resulted in him being sent to prison for two and a half years, along with a number of others in a scandal which had involved the National Crime Agency, the Gambling Commission and the Football Association, following two separate newspaper investigations.

Born in Huddersfield in 1980, Delroy Facey joined his hometown team as an apprentice at 16-years-old and signed his first senior contract with the club the following year. By the 1998-99 season, he was breaking into their first team.

His playing career became somewhat nomadic. In total, he played for ten different clubs and spent time on loan at three others, meaning that, although he ended up making 451 senior appearances in the EFL and Premier League - where he played ten times for Bolton Wanderers between 2002 and 2004 - between 1997 and 2012, the 85 times he played for Huddersfield was the most that he played for any one club.

The only silverware of his career came at the end of the 2009-10 season. He'd joined Notts County in 2008 and became something of a fan favourite, and in 2010 he was part of their team which won the League Two title, although he was released by the club at the end of that season.

He also found a route international game as a result of his success at club level. He qualified to play for Grenada, Trinidad & Tobago and Jamaica through his parents and was called up to the Grenada squad in June 2009. Between then and 2011, he made twelve appearances for the Grenada national team.

Football League World looks at ten former EFL players to have served time behind bars...

The scandal broke in December 2013. Separate investigations carried out by The Sunday Times and The Sun on Sunday had revealed a spot-fixing scandal within the EFL, leading to the arrest of six people, including Facey. Spot-fixing - the practice of gambling on any aspect of a match apart from the final result - had become a signficant issue in line with the growth of online gambling, which allowed a wider range of options for betting than had ever been seen before.

It was reported that Sam Sodje, a former player for Reading and Portsmouth, claimed that he could arrange for footballers in the Football League Championship to get themselves booked in exchange for cash payments. It was also reported that Sodje "was filmed describing how he had twice punched an opponent, Oldham Athletic's Jose Baxter, while playing for Portsmouth in a League One match last February as he sought to receive a red card in exchange for £70,000."

Facey was charged with conspiracy to bribe alongside Moses Swaibu, a non-league player who'd played for a number of clubs including Lincoln City and Bromley. The case went to trial in the middle of April 2015. The court heard that Facey had told a footballer at Hyde FC that he could make some "easy money" by fixing the result of a match, and had also told a contact that some Football Conference - now the National League - teams would "do" a game in return for payment.

And on the 29th April 2015, the verdict came in. Delroy Facey was convicted of conspiracy to bribe and sentenced to two and a half years in prison. The BBC reported at the time that, after the sentencing, "Andy Young, from the CPS, said Facey had acted as the "middleman", sourcing players who would be willing to fix matches." Swaibu was also convicted and sentenced to 16 months in prison.

The temptation to lower division and non-league footballers is clear. The very nature of spot-fixing can make it difficult to prove, and the very existence of it is a direct result of the proliferation of online gambling in the first decade of the 21st century. The FA tightened their rules regarding players and gambling in August 2014, but for Delroy Facey the damage had been done. His reputation was in tatters, with the memories of him as a player fundamentally tarnished by the knowledge that he'd been involved in such disgraceful behaviour at the time.

10 Abilities That Ruin Good Pokémon

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Aden Carter is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2020. He specializes in lists and guides, with a focus on RPGs, horror games, survival horror, Soulslikes, cozy/sim games, and action-adventure titles. Before joining DualShockers, Aden contributed to GameRant, XDA, Gamepur, I

There are some amazing Pokémon out there that are great for both playing through the game and using them in competitive battles. These Pokémon are often the go-to for those who like to play competitively.

However, there are also Pokémon that are good, but they are absolutely ruined because they have a bad ability. Think of Pokémon like Slaking, who has a high base stat total, but the fact that it has the Truant ability makes it so it can only attack every other turn.

Otherwise, this Pokémon is just loafing around. There are several Pokémon like this that would be amazing if it wasn’t for a terrible ability holding it back.

Swampert is a great Pokémon, and one my go-to starter Pokémon line in Ruby and Sapphire. It has solid stats, with it being a good attacker who only gets better when it Mega Evolves.

The only thing lacking for Swampert is its speed, as it only has 60 speed, but the rest of its stats more than make up for its lack of quickness. Unfortunately, it has two abilities that somewhat ruin it. These abilities are Torrent and Damp.

Damp just prevents explosions, and Torrent increases Swampert’s water-type moves, but only when it is low in health. Thankfully, Mega Swampert gets Swift Swim, which doubles its speed in the rain.

I mentioned it in the intro, but it needed to be an entry on this list, because Slaking is one of the all time greatest Pokémon stats-wise, but its ability makes it so it can’t do anything.

If you weren’t aware, Slaking has a base stat total of 670, which means that it rivals legendary Pokémon like Rayquaza. With power like that, Nintendo and Game Freak needed a way to make it so Slaking didn’t destroy the world, so they gave it Truant.

Truant makes it so it has to loaf around every other turn, leaving it open to attack and set-up moves. This ability absolutely destroys Slaking, making it a complete pass in most circumstances.

Regigigas is a powerhouse, and there is no denying it. With an attack stat of 160, this Pokémon dominates with physical attacks. However, it is ruined by its horrible ability, Slow Start.

Slow Start may sound like it simply slows a Pokémon down, and that is partly true, but it is worse than that. This ability makes it so the Pokémon’s speed and attack stats are both halved for the first five turns of battle.

Five turns is a ridiculously long time to have half of your attack, and it basically ruins Regigigas, as it can be defeated within those first five turns by any competent trainer. Just bring out a special attacker, and Regigigas doesn’t stand much of a chance.

A complementary Ability can turn a weak, stat-challenged Pokemon into a force to be reckoned with and here are the best.

Machamp is a fan-favorite Pokémon that is an absolute powerhouse. It rocks an overall base stat total of 505, with a high attack stat, decent defense, and decent HP. Unfortunately, it is brought down by the ability No Guard.

No Guard was the signature ability of the Machamp line prior to Generation V, and it makes it so enemy and player attacks always land. This means that accuracy suddenly doesn’t matter as both Pokémon will always hit their target.

This means that even 1-hit KO moves are guaranteed to land, and if Machamp is outsped, then it is curtains for this Pokémon. It doesn’t help too much that Machamp also has Guts, which increases its attack by 50%, but only when it has a status condition.

To be fair, Illuminate isn’t the worst ability out there, but it has absolutely no effect in battle, making it completely useless to have on a strong Pokémon. Starmie is one such Pokémon that has a useless ability.

Instead of Illuminate affecting anything in battle, it simply increases your encounter rate outside of battle. This is okay whenever you are trying to farm for a specific Pokémon, but it is otherwise useless.

It would be nice for this to be a hidden ability on Starmie instead of its primary ability alongside Natural Cure (which has a little more value). Thankfully, it gets the ability Huge Power whenever it Mega Evolves, as this amplifies its power.

Imagine that you caught a Wimpod. You stuck with it despite its flaws, raised it to level 30, and you were rewarded with it evolving into Golisopod. This is a significant power boost. After all, it has a base stat total of 530.

Unfortunately, the ability Wimp Out, which is the main ability of Wimpod, has changed into Emergency Exit. While the name of the ability has changed, the effect of the ability has not, making it so Golisopod will switch out whenever its health falls below 50%.

This ability isn’t the worst, but it does make using Golisopod a bit of a nuisance. After all, it may be a great match-up against a Pokémon you are fighting, but then it switches out because its health got too low.

From Ultra Necrozma’s stat boosts to Volo’s grueling final battle, these Pokémon encounters bend the rules to deliver memorable challenges.

Gallade is a great Pokémon that matches the 518 base stat total of Gardevoir, is psychic and fighting type, and hits like a truck with its 125 attack stat. However, the ability Steadfast is pretty pointless.

Steadfast is an ability that increases the Pokémon’s speed by one stage whenever it flinches. This is an okay ability, but it doesn’t come into effect very much, which is unfortunate since Gallade doesn’t have the best speed.

It has an 80 speed stat, which means there are a lot of Pokémon that will outspeed it. However, they have to specifically use flinching moves to make Gallade’s speed increase, and if that doesn’t happen, then the ability never gets used, making it a complete waste.

Rhyperior is a hefty Pokémon with a base stat total of 535, making it very powerful, especially with an attack stat of 140. It also boasts high defense, with a score of 130, and great health that has a score of 115. It is a tank, and it needs to be since its speed stat is only 40.

However, the worst part about Rhyperior is the ability Lightning Rod. This ability makes it so any single-target eclectic attacks are redirected to Rhyperior. Since Rhyperior is a ground-type it isn’t affected by electric attacks.

However, the only way this ability is effective is if you are doing double-battles. After all, nobody would dare bring out an electric-type Pokémon against a ground type, unless the sprinklers are on. Solid Rock does help its defences, but with those pesky x4 weaknesses, it's not going to do enough.

Archeops is coming in with a staggering 567 base stat total. This is very impressive, especially when you look at its attack and speed stats, which happen to be 140 and 110. It hits fast, and it hits hard.

Unfortunately, Archeops is a bit of a Defeatist, and by that, I mean it has the ability Defeatist. This horrible ability makes it so that the Pokémon’s attack and special attack are halved whenever its health falls below 50%.

One solid hit on Archeops that takes it below 50% health, and suddenly its attack is heavily nerfed. It wouldn’t be so bad if it could fall back on its special attack, but that is also halved.

Camerupt is a solid Pokémon with a base stat total of 460. This isn’t as strong as some of the other Pokémon on the list, but it is up there. It has both a solid special attack and attack, with them being 105 and 100.

As is the case with every Pokémon on this list, it has an ability that is pretty much useless. It gets Magma Armor, which prevents the Pokémon from becoming frozen. While this sounds good, you have to think that Camerupt is a fire type Pokémon.

If you know type effectiveness, then you know that fire-type Pokémon are super effective against ice types. In other words, nobody would dare use an ice-type move against Camerupt, even if it doesn't resist it because of its secondary ground typing. Solid Rock serves it better, though again its 4x weakness to water will usually be too much with or without it.

This list includes some Pokémon that are stronger than their evolutionary forms.

Box Office: ‘You Can See Everything’ Hits $80 Million, Becoming A24’s Third-Biggest Domestic Release

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

“You Can See Everything,” an intimate look at Theranos founder Elizabeth Holmes, has earned nearly $80 million at the domestic box office, becoming A24‘s third-highest-grossing release in North America. It bypasses “Everything Everywhere All at Once” and its $77 million haul to take its spot on the indie studio’s chart, and that film won the Oscar for Best Picture.

With $79.8 million stateside, “You Can See Everything” sits behind the summer blockbuster “Backrooms” ($197 million) and 2025’s “Marty Supreme” ($96 million) on the list of A24 releases. Other A24 films made more globally, with “Backrooms” earning more than $400 million, “Marty Supreme” grossing $192.4 million, and “Everything Everywhere All at Once” netting $147.9 million. Related Stories Box Office: ‘Godzilla Minus Zero’ Thunders with $31.6 Million, Scoring the Biggest Debut Ever for a Live-Action, Foreign Language Film Dylan Skolnick Returning as Co-Director of the Cinema Arts Centre Following Protests and Boycotts

However, “You Can See Everything” has yet to be released in most international markets. It’s unclear if Holmes’ fraud will resonate with moviegoers so far from Silicon Valley. It doesn’t matter if it doesn’t connect, because with a budget of $7 million, “You Can See Everything” is already wildly profitable. Its success means that Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim, the directing duo behind the doc, are now in high demand.

“You Can See Everything” also jumped ahead of “March of the Penguins” ($77 million) on the list of highest-grossing documentaries in North America. Variety reports that makes it the second highest-grossing doc behind Michael Moore’s “Fahrenheit 9/11,” which earned $119 million domestically. That doesn’t account for music documentaries like “Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour” ($267.1 million) and “Michael Jackson’s This is It” ($268 million), which were far more popular.

A24 is on a hot streak. In addition to “Backrooms,” the indie studio has released hits like “The Drama” and “Primetime.” Its latest film, “Club Kid,” grossed an impressive $390,583 this weekend when it opened on six screens in New York and Los Angeles. That’s a per-screen average of $65,097. A24 acquired the drama about a hard-living gay man and his son out of the Cannes Film Festival, where it debuted to raves. The studio hopes to position the film and its writer, director, and star Jordan Firstman in the Oscar race.

Anya Taylor-Joy Goes Vintage for the LACMA Gala in Pierre Cardin Ruffles

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Last night, the film, fashion, art, and entertainment worlds came together for the Los Angeles County Museum of Art (LACMA)’s 15th annual Art+Film Gala—celebrating the groundbreaking work of industry leaders such as Dune filmmaker Denis Villeneuve and painter Vija Celmins, known for her photo-realistic explorations of nature. And among the star-studded list of attendees, from Kylie Jenner and Kim Kardashian to Nicole Kidman and Rosie Huntington-Whiteley, was Dune’s own Anya Taylor-Joy, who showed up to support her director in a striking dose of winter whites.

Styled by Law Roach, the actor went vintage for the occasion, digging through the Pierre Cardin archives for a shimmering Spring/Summer 1997 ensemble. For a base layer, the look featured a sleek and simple silver slip dress, held up by delicate straps and adorned all over with subtle embroidery. The maxi was wrapped in a dramatic, voluminous coat, fashioned from layers of organza stitched into oversize ruffles. It created a spiral effect that brought the eye down to her matching open-toe Jimmy Choo platforms, and back up to her celestial Tiffany & Co. diamond necklace.

Taylor-Joy pulled her hair into a slicked-back bun and gave her eyelids an icy shimmer. Diamond studs finished things off, and she posed for photos with Villeneuve in front of Chris Burden’s public Urban Light installation—a collection of oversize street lamps that doubled as a red carpet for the evening.

The annual fundraising event, co-chaired once again by Leonardo DiCaprio and LACMA trustee Eva Chow, supported the museum’s efforts to expand its programming, making film more central to its exhibitions and education.

Battlefield 6 player sues EA after permanent ban and wins to get account back

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

A Battlefield 6 player says he took EA to court after being permanently banned from the shooter and won, with the judge ordering the publisher to reverse the sanction and restore access to his account.

Reddit user Rychadv shared the update in r/Battlefield on November 8, claiming EA could not support the reason for his ban.

Rychadv’s case began with a post in r/electronicarts. He said he had returned to Battlefield 6 after a long break to check out Season 4 and was greeted by a ban notification when he launched the game. He denied using cheats, exploits or inappropriate chat, and said his appeal produced no useful explanation beyond three dots.

The player later wrote that EA told him the ban was for cheating, prompting him to say, “I’d like to see them prove that.” He then said he filed a lawsuit in Brazil and submitted evidence from Windows showing that no illegal software or unauthorized devices had been used on his PC.

Rychadv said he used Brazil’s Juizado Especial Cível, a lower-complexity civil court process similar to small claims court.

He said the filing came with no court fees or taxes and that he represented himself because he is a lawyer. Brazilian court guidance describes these proceedings as covering lower-complexity civil and consumer disputes, with cases generally free to file.

He also claimed EA appeared in the case and submitted a defense, but made several claims without evidence to support them. According to Rychadv, the ruling restored his account, ordered compensation and imposed a fine on EA. He did not disclose the amount of either payment.

In follow-up comments, Rychadv confirmed that EA had already unbanned him and that his account was “back to normal.” He also said the legal process cost him nothing, while EA’s total payment was roughly four times the price of the game, plus the fine

Battlefield 6 devs respond after players hit with missing content bug

Battlefield 6 player gets Steam refund after 470 hours because their favorite mode was removed

Battlefield 2042 players call for EA boycott after failed refund petition

EA’s public rules add context to the dispute. The publisher says Battlefield 6 uses its Javelin anti-cheat system and lists third-party software, exploits and other unfair advantages as violations. EA also says it requires clear evidence of cheating to ban a player.

Its account support page directs players to the Penalty History tool to review penalties and submit appeals, with removing a penalty listed as a possible outcome when a mistake is found.

The public Reddit and X posts do not include a case number or a link to a court docket. No EA response appears in the posts reviewed for this story, so the court result is currently based on Rychadv’s account and follow-up comments.

A Rare $7M Double Townhome Hits the Market in DC—Complete With a Heated Terrace and ‘Fortress’ Garage

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The word "compound" is very rarely associated with townhouses in bustling city neighborhoods, but a $7 million property in Washington DC’s Dupont Circle might just be the exception.

Offered together, the connected luxury residences at 1617 and 1619 19th Street NW are an incredibly rare opportunity to own some serious square footage in one of the capital’s most vibrant areas.

The property is anchored by 1619—a four-bedroom, 4.5-bathroom residence that spans four impeccably-renovated levels. Next door, 1617 serves as a complementary rowhome with three fully-leased residences—a spacious one bedroom with a private patio, and two additional two-bedroom, two-bathroom units.

Spanning 6,600 square feet and boasting nine bedrooms and 9.5 half-bathrooms between them, the pair of buildings has "all kinds of unique use cases," according to listing agent Barak Sky with Serhant.

Sky explains that there are plenty of impressive features that could make this the perfect situation for a "needle in a haystack buyer who can’t find exactly what they need."

Perhaps a notable public figure would like to reside in the four-level residence, and dedicate one of the smaller units in the secondary building to a round-the-clock security team. A family could take advantage of the opportunity to create a multigenerational home.

Maybe it’s just right for a buyer who wants to live in a gorgeous home and generate rental income. The three units currently pull in $136,200 annually, according to the listing.

“It’s definitely special,” says Sky. “We thought this was a unique opportunity to present this compound as one offering.”

Aside from the fact that the sheer volume of space is ripe with possibility, there’s another feature of the property that brings endless potential.

“The detached garage is completely like a fortress,” Sky adds.

A steel two-car garage is structurally engineered for future expansion, and could be used to add a private, elevated pool deck or even an additional accessory residence. Given the historic nature of the property, which was built in 1910, the chance to add to the building’s legacy is certainly exciting.

Should the buyer choose to leave everything as-is, they’re still in for a treat.

The primary residence has been painstakingly renovated, and features an “uncompromising commitment to craftsmanship,” according to the listing, which touts the dwelling’s “soaring ceilings and extensive custom millwork.”

A private elevator services each floor, connecting the sprawling formal spaces, gourmet kitchen, and inviting family room to the private retreats upstairs.

An entire level is dedicated to the primary suite, which includes a private terrace, dual walk-in closets, and a spa-inspired marble bathroom equipped with heated floors, a steam shower, and a sauna.

Of the additional bedrooms, two are ensuites.

The lower level hosts a recreation lounge, a temperature-controlled wine cellar, a guest suite, and space for what could become an efficiency kitchen.

The outdoor space is just as lavish, and is described as a “spectacular private sanctuary” in the listing. There’s a wood-burning fireplace, outdoor audio and television, heat lamps for the winter, a misting system for the summer, and heated walkways.

There are also a number of seemingly invisible amenities that were carefully selected to make life more comfortable, such as seamlessly-integrated smart home technology and whole-house water filtration.

As for the location, this gem is located on one of Dupont Circle’s "most coveted tree-lined blocks,”"according to the listing.

“This exceptional offering places the very best of Washington at your doorstep, from acclaimed restaurants, boutique shopping, and neighborhood cafes to Embassy Row, downtown business districts, cultural institutions, and Metro access,” it reads.

Not Butter Or Cheese: The Creamy Ingredient That Makes Grilled Cheese 10x Richer

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Grilled cheese is already a decadent meal that requires little effort, but a different form of richness from meat, rather than cheese, can make it more complexly delicious. If you're going to reach for rich meat, then look no further than pâté, something that comes in enough variety to suit any cheese combination you choose for your sandwich.

Many of us are familiar with pâté on a charcuterie board, served with a light cracker so you can experience its full flavors. But it's a common sandwich ingredient — take, for example, French influence in the Vietnamese bánh mì, creating an international grilled cheese style that's as unique as it is delicious. As it heats, the pâté practically melts like a meaty butter, infusing your cheese and bread with all that richness and delivering a truly powerful taste. When done properly, the pâté should mix directly into the gooey cheese, creating a single filling with the best possible texture.

Thanks to its high fat content, pâté has a signature silky texture that's hard to find elsewhere. Even vegetarian varieties made from mushrooms, soft cheese like ricotta, and butter can be a spot of decadence on an otherwise straightforward meal. It has a remarkable capacity for taking even the most underrated cheeses and turning them into gooey perfection, even if they don't have much moisture or fat to begin with. Combined with a sturdy bread toasted with butter or mayo, each layer of your grilled cheese will end up impossibly rich.

Pâté is an extraordinarily delicate ingredient, so adding it to any hot recipe takes a bit of know-how. Some are designed to be eaten hot or cold, so selecting the right variety and application can prevent you from losing out on any of its delicious virtues. For example, a terrine — not to be confused with true pâté or even rillettes — warms beautifully.

Heating up a pâté made to be eaten cold may make it greasy and fall apart, rather than melt. Instead of these, look for ones used in pâté en croûte, a type of fluffy pastry that uses it as a filling. Just like a country variety, aka terrine, it has a coarser texture that adds a satisfying bite to your sandwich and won't split once heated. These can also come in a dizzying variety, from meaty ones seasoned with nothing but salt to ones laden with chopped vegetables, nuts, and spices.

A simple smear on one side of your bread does the trick just fine, and the direct contact with the bread lets it soak up excess fat as it heats. But if you use an aged or low-fat cheese, try incorporating it with another simple trick for a meltier result: grating the cheese first. By tossing the cheese with your pâté, you coat each individual strand with a bit of the fat and ensure that, when they melt, the two ingredients fuse together. If you use slices, it can also help to smear a bit of pâté on each one before layering them between your bread.

Cloudflare CEO Likens AI Agent Optimization To A New Version Of SEO via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Calls from ChatGPT and Claude often get filed as direct. This checklist shows how to track AI search as its own call source.

Calls from ChatGPT and Claude often get filed as direct. This checklist shows how to track AI search as its own call source.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Guide: AI Max Drove 68% Of Retail Invalid Clicks

Cloudflare's CEO says OpenAI pays for hints that tell its crawler when pages change, and sites may charge agents less when they explain their visits.

Cloudflare recorded its first revenue from what it calls Act 4 products in the third quarter, co-founder and CEO Matthew Prince said on the company’s earnings call. Act 4 covers the products meant to help create a new business model for the internet.

He also said OpenAI is paying for hints that tell its crawler when web pages change. For customers that want more traffic from AI agents, Cloudflare is helping with what he described as “sort of the new version of SEO.”

Prince gave no figure for Act 4 revenue on the call. Cloudflare’s third-quarter results release, which reported revenue of $782.5 million, up 39% year over year, doesn’t break it out either.

A Goldman Sachs analyst asked when it will be time for Act 4 products to generate meaningful revenue. Prince replied that Cloudflare is already “recognizing significant amounts of revenue” due to the efficiency it brings to AI agents’ visits to websites. As an example, he pointed to the partnership with OpenAI that was explained during Cloudflare’s August earnings call.

He also described how under the partnership, Cloudflare provides OpenAI with information as to when a webpage has been updated (for example, a restaurant updates their pricing), so OpenAI’s crawler doesn’t have to keep going back and re-fetching the same webpage.

“OpenAI is paying for that,” he said, adding that he expects other AI companies to do the same.

Cloudflare made this public on July 8, in a research pilot using signals from the websites participating (for example, content freshness and page changes). In its Oct. 15 results posting, the company said it was still working with OpenAI to determine how better discovery could lead to business outcomes (I wrote about this on Oct. 20).

Prince did not indicate during his call what OpenAI is paying for this relationship or when the payments started.

Asked by a Wells Fargo analyst whether Cloudflare customers want more traffic from AI agents, Prince said the answer depends on the customer, and that some want as many agents as possible.

“Others see them as a bane to their business and want to keep them away,” he said.

Cloudflare helps the customers who want agents draw more of them and handle the traffic efficiently, according to Prince. Customers who see agents as a threat get them blocked, and they aren’t charged for that traffic. He split customers the same way on the August call.

An agent choosing where its user eats dinner might pull menus from 1,000 restaurant websites, he said, and the user goes to one. The other 999 restaurants carry a cost with no benefit. A person might look at two or three pages, and the scale with agents is far higher.

On the August call, Prince said that if current trends continued, nonhuman traffic would be as much as 1,000 times human traffic within five years. This time, he said he expects it to happen sooner.

Traffic on that scale would need more bandwidth and more servers. “It can’t be a free ride for all the agents that are out there,” Prince said.

Beyond efficiency deals like OpenAI’s, Prince expects some form of micropayments when agents visit sites. The more an agent tells a site about why it’s there, the more a site may be willing to “charge it less to access the page,” he said.

In his example, an agent tells a travel site it’s planning a Hawaii vacation for a family of four. The site might let that agent in for free because the request opens up advertising opportunities.

He isn’t sure how long it will be before that becomes a major source of revenue. Looking 10 years out, he said, “I would imagine that this becomes the largest part of Cloudflare’s business.”

Cloudflare moved two payment products, the Monetization Gateway and Pay Per Use, into beta on Sept. 30. The Gateway lets sellers charge AI agents per request, and Pay Per Use pays publishers who accept an AI company’s offer for reported uses.

The one Act 4 payment Prince described in detail goes to Cloudflare for making OpenAI’s crawling more efficient. Whether the websites in the OpenAI pilot get a share wasn’t addressed on the call.

Prince laid out a choice, to welcome agents or block them, that is currently possible within Cloudflare’s dashboard. Cloudflare offers AI Crawl Control on every plan. This allows users to permit or deny access to individual AI crawlers, and its documentation lists the Pay Per Crawl option as being in a private beta. The CEO didn’t mention a single Cloudflare service to optimize websites for agent traffic during the call.

Prince expects Cloudflare to talk a lot more about its Act 4 products “in the quarters to come.”

Keep an eye on whether other AI companies follow OpenAI’s lead and pay for crawl hints, as Prince predicted. It’ll also be worth seeing whether Cloudflare’s next disclosures explain how much of that money reaches the sites whose pages the agents use.

Mark Schwarzer has praised Liverpool youngster Víctor Munoz for his impressive contribution off the bench during the Reds’ 3-1 Premier League victory over Crystal Palace.

The former Chelsea and Fulham goalkeeper highlighted the substitute’s impact after he played a crucial role in Alexander Isak’s 89th-minute goal at Selhurst Park.

Liverpool had entered the contest under pressure following consecutive defeats against Arsenal and Fenerbahce, but goals from Alexis Mac Allister, Florian Wirtz and Isak ensured Andoni Iraola’s side returned to winning ways.

While the three goalscorers will attract plenty of attention, Schwarzer was particularly impressed by Munoz, whose introduction provided Liverpool with fresh attacking impetus.

Speaking on BBC Radio 5 Live, Schwarzer singled out Muñoz for his contribution to Liverpool’s third goal.

The youngster caused problems for Crystal Palace down the right flank, using his pace and movement to create space in dangerous areas.

His involvement proved decisive in the closing stages as Liverpool produced an excellent attacking move to put the result beyond doubt.

“Another really good goal from Liverpool. Victor Munoz down the right hand side, he’s made a difference since he came on.

“He played the ball across the box, Florian Wirtz does well to lay it off to Alexander Isak, who picks his spot and puts it past Dean Henderson. Liverpool deserve it.”

The young forward advanced down the right before delivering a dangerous ball into the penalty area.

Wirtz demonstrated excellent awareness to lay the ball off for Isak, who produced a composed finish beyond Palace goalkeeper Dean Henderson.

It was a well-worked goal involving three Liverpool attackers, but Schwarzer felt Muñoz deserved particular credit for the difference he made following his introduction.

His direct running provided Liverpool with another attacking outlet and helped create the opportunity for Isak to score.

Alexander Isak’s late finish at Selhurst Park capped off another clinical performance from the Swedish international.

The 89th-minute strike marked Isak’s 7th Premier League goal of the campaign, moving him within just two goals of Manchester City’s Erling Haaland (9 goals) at the top of the Golden Boot standings.

Although Isak has not always been at his most influential in Liverpool’s overall attacking play, his goalscoring record remains impressive.

With seven league goals already to his name, the striker remains firmly in contention near the top of the Premier League scoring charts.

The 3-1 triumph at Selhurst Park comes at a crucial moment for Andoni Iraola’s side.

The Reds suffered a frustrating 1-0 defeat against Arsenal at Anfield before losing 3-2 to Fenerbahçe in the Champions League.

Those results increased the pressure on Iraola’s side, who needed a positive response before the November international break.

Mac Allister opened the scoring against Palace with an excellent header from Dominik Szoboszlai’s delivery in the 40th minute.

Wirtz then doubled Liverpool’s advantage on the hour mark, finishing a superb attacking move involving Ronald Araújo and Bradley Barcola.

Isak made it three to seal the win for Iraola before a contentious penalty right before full time gave Palace a consolation goal.

Franklin Templeton Will Use OpenAI ChatGPT to Predict Crypto

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

In early November 2026, OpenAI published a detailed customer story highlighting how Franklin Templeton, the global asset manager overseeing roughly $1.8 trillion in assets, is integrating advanced AI tools into its investment research process.

The firm is not simply experimenting with chatbots for note-taking. It is systematically building AI-powered workflows to help portfolio managers compare competing research views, surface overlooked risks, and make better-informed investment decisions.

According to Katrina Dudley, Senior Vice President of Public Market Investments at Franklin Templeton, the biggest unlock has been merging continuous intelligence with human judgment.

Earlier attempts at AI involved feeding data into a database and connecting models to it, but the results fell short because the models lacked context about how the data interrelated. The breakthrough came when the firm added agents and a semantic layer, a structured model that defines what the firm’s data means and how different pieces connect.

Without shared meaning, AI keeps guessing. Franklin Templeton only got useful research agents after they built a semantic layer — what the data means and how it connects — before scaling. Define meaning first, then let the agents run. https://t.co/3Ju9nXtdW1

Franklin Templeton is utilizing OpenAI’s Codex to create a “connected intelligence layer” that integrates inputs from research, risk, compliance, and trading teams, providing portfolio managers with a comprehensive view of investment ideas. This new workflow emphasizes continuous intelligence, allowing analysts and managers to engage with data in real time.

Dudley highlights the firm’s commitment to avoiding the filter-bubble issue common in consumer AI by ensuring tools present diverse opinions and risks. Franklin Templeton adopts a structured approach to deployment, pairing technical experts with practitioners in internal hackathons.

Promising projects undergo security reviews and expert support before production. The firm encourages experimentation within clear risk boundaries, recognizing that not every initiative will succeed, but each should yield valuable insights.

Ultimately, the goal is to create a governed, reusable intelligence foundation that allows teams to focus on gathering insights while AI handles synthesis and pattern detection. This initiative is part of the firm’s broader digital-assets strategy, including proactive moves in crypto investments.

As the largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization, Bitcoin attracts a vast audience of holders and investors. Bitcoin Hyper aims to leverage this liquidity through a Layer 2 network designed to facilitate faster transactions and decentralized finance (DeFi) functionality.

Bitcoin Hyper’s architecture uses the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM), bringing Solana-style execution capabilities to a Bitcoin-centric ecosystem.

The goal is straightforward: combine Bitcoin’s substantial liquidity and established investor base with transaction speeds suitable for modern decentralized applications. Key features include near-instant finality and low transaction costs.

HYPER is also gaining attention due to its presale pricing. The project has reportedly raised over $33M, indicating strong early fundraising momentum. Additionally, it offers 35% APY staking rewards, incentivizing early participants to stake their tokens and earn more HYPER instead of leaving their allocations idle.

With the planned launch date of November 26 approaching, the presale is entering a crucial phase. Investors seeking exposure to Bitcoin’s expanding Layer 2 ecosystem are eagerly anticipating Hyper’s market debut before it gets listed on centralized exchanges.

Hungarian VW Bus Goes from ‘Matchbox: The Movie’ to Matchbox, the Toy Car

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

A 1976 Volkswagen T2 achieves fame and immortality.

Filmed in Budapest and Bratislava, Matchbox: The Movie has a fairly tenuous connection to the little die-cast toys. Starring the always charismatic John Cena as a CIA agent racing to deal with a rogue nuclear weapon, the central conceit is that a group of friends who used to play together with Matchbox cars in middle school reunite in adulthood to take down the bad guys.

It’s a movie that plays like a kid narrating a story while pushing die-cast cars around the carpet, which feels like a pretty good fit for the brand association. Compared to Hot Wheels, which are all about speed and pulling off loops on those iconic orange tracks, Matchbox as a brand skews a little more realistic, and often features construction machines and vintage vehicles.

See all results for used cars for sale near 17402

Speaking of iconic orange, one of the central vehicles onscreen is a 1970s Volkswagen T2, the beloved Microbus. These things are such a huge part of popular culture that one was the main plot point in an episode of Futurama, which saw the crew hopping into a VW Bus and following Beck’s band around. (Best joke: When asked by Professor Farnsworth where the onboard time dilation device is, Fry reaches under the seat and produces a bong.)

In this case, two VW T2s were used in filming, both owned by Budapest-based Dávid Sipos. One of the two needed sufficient modification for stunt driving that it ended up no longer road legal, but the more original of the pair survived the filming schedule unscathed and came back into Sipos’s possession. As a tie-in with the film, Mattel of course produced Matchbox models of the film cars, and Sipos was delighted to find that the blister-pack model of the VW Bus was a faithful match for his car, right down to the aftermarket sunroof and identical Hungarian license plate.

The story gets even better, with the involvement of the original owner who picked up the keys to this orange bus some 50 years ago. If a VW Microbus is a symbol of counterculture in the U.S., that’s not the case overseas. In 1964, reacting to European tariffs on U.S. chicken products, President Lyndon B. Johnson enacted the so-called “chicken tax,” slapping a 25 percent penalty on various products including imported European light trucks.

For Volkswagen, this meant the death knell of U.S.-exported T2 pickups and panel vans, so the Microbus was typically a passenger van stateside. In Europe, working T2s continued chugging along as part of the furniture of a busy city, getting people and goods where they needed to go.

See all results for used cars for sale near 17402

This particular 1976 T2B was delivered by train to Budapest for the Hungarian Water Works to use as a service vehicle. A young employee with the last name Thaly drove it right off the rail car at the depot, and it entered fleet service for the next eight years.

By 1984, something more modern was needed, so the company offered the VW to Thaly, who promptly snapped it up. He kept working, but the T2 effectively enjoyed a sunny retirement, stored carefully in a garage and used as a summertime camping vehicle driven throughout Europe, first with his children, then with his grandchildren. This went on for three decades.

In more recent times, Sipos was working for an advertising agency that often landed accounts for youth-oriented brands. “Somehow I was always the one suggesting that we should have a VW Bus covered in the brand’s graphics as part of the campaign,” he says.

The company eventually bought a slightly earlier 1972 T2A for permanent use as a rolling billboard. Far from just being a work vehicle, employees started borrowing it outside work and just driving it around, as the experience was so much fun.

“There’s something about traveling in a VW Bus that instantly creates a sense of community,” Sipos says.

The T2 became something of a signature of the agency, with friends and colleagues often flagging likely-looking vehicles up for sale. One day in 2013, Thaly’s beloved orange VW cropped up in a listing, and while the advertising company didn’t need it in the fleet, Sipos bought it as a personal vehicle.

This T2 ended up onscreen when Matchbox's director, Sam Hargrave, spotted a photo of it. Sipos also had a second, very similar T2 at the time, green in color, and it was the latter that was cloned to look like the 1976 T2B and modified for stunt work.

The story of how the actual Hungarian registration ended up on the toy car is a bit of a mystery, as the onscreen cars used different license plates. Mattel’s designers would have had piles of reference photos to comb through, and somehow it was settled that the detail on the plate would be DJN-623, the plate this van has been wearing since 1993.

Sipos made sure to get this unexpectedly accurate model of the Thaly family’s beloved orange Volkswagen to them, and he keeps in close contact with them. How typical of a Volkswagen Bus to still be bringing people together, 50 years after it hit the road.

Breaking Down the ‘Black Doves’ Season 2 Ending

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

For all the international stakes of the Black Doves season 2 finale, the tensest moments really came down to family. Can any spy and/or assassin have a family, found or otherwise? Won’t they all have to kill or be killed in the end? Here’s where Helen (Kiera Knightley), Sam (Ben Whishaw), and Reed (Sarah Lancashire) stand at the end of Black Doves season 2.

This season, while Helen starts to wonder that she’s been sidelined as a “spy wife” and abandoned by Reed, Sam has been given a peculiar kill list by an organization called Kestrel. He has to check it off in order to avoid jail time, or worse, but the names all seem to be connected to the Black Doves of old. This leads Sam and Helen to learn more about Reed, their employer. Then, we learn in final episode that the last person on Sam’s list is Reed herself.

The finale drags out the penultimate episode’s cliffhanger at the beginning of the finale. As it turns out, even though Reed was the fourth and final person on Sam’s list, he did not kill her. He turned her in to Patrick (Sam Riley) alive. That was enough to satisfy his contract, pay his debt, keep him out of jail, and destroy the evidence that Patrick has against Helen. Allegedly, that is! I don’t trust him...

Sam then attends the funeral of Jerry (Samuel Barnett), the concierge he was sleeping with and had to murder because of this mess. There is truly no rest for the wicked.

The rival bird-themed agency that Patrick works for easily recruits the rogue Black Dove. She’s anxious to cause chaos again after getting sidelined to a desk gig.

Oh right... there is a political storyline on this show. It’s easy to forget that when most of the action revolves around spies and assassins who don’t necessarily color inside partisan lines. Ultimately, Helen’s husband’s summit didn’t even happen. Kestrel agents attacked the summit in order to kidnap the US Defense envoy Cecille (Neve Campbell), get Helen in a car alone with Wallace, and orchestrate a confrontation between Reed and Katia Chernov at the Black Doves headquarters.

Big twist alert! We learn in the finale that the Alexi Chernov’s wife is Reed’s long lost daughter “Vita” who she abandoned in the Soviet Union. She’s been secretly plotting this for a long time and aims to get back on her mother and expose the Black Doves. Unfortunately, she neither gets her revenge nor lives to try again. The trio of female assassins–Laila, Williams, and Eleanor–arrive to rescue Reed to get her out.

Katia gives Helen an ultimatum: kill the Prime Minister or their children’s nanny Marie, a Black Dove turncoat, will kill them. (We knew that Marie was associated with Reed at the end of season 1... but both I and Wikipedia thought she was her daughter. Egg on our face, eh?) That’s the only way, Katia says, to put an end to all of this. Seems unfair to Wallace, who as far as we know has done nothing wrong, but whatever. Jerry was also an innocent victim this season.

But Helen does not shoot her husband. Instead, she leaves him in a car park and drives away with Sam to rescue their twins while the other assassins are saving Reed. She does so with a decent amount of ease. Helen shoots Marie point-blank in the head, but then takes her children to safety.

The final episode certainly has its casualties. Katia kills Cecille, sure. I’ve already mentioned that Marie dies, and Katia as well. Dani also dies by grenade in an elevator. But more main characters survived than you may have expected.

At the end of the episode, Patrick stages a meet-cute with Helen and slips her his business card. It’s absolutely a threat. But Reed and Helen are reunited. She also sits down with Wallace and prepares to tell him some version of the truth.

How will he take it? Will Helen and Reed manage to use their skills for good? And will Sam manage to stay in Italy for good this time? Just about all of his friends have contracts to kill him... but what if they just ignore them for a while? Will Eleanor and Williams stay together? We’re going to need Black Doves season 3 to find out.

De Rossi praises Genoa’s performance, Amorim as a head coach and Milan’s ‘world class’ duo

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Daniele De Rossi has revealed that he congratulated his Genoa players despite their defeat against AC Milan this afternoon.

Milan did what they had to do after their midweek setback against Ferencvaros at San Siro by securing a much-needed three points to move back into the top four in the Serie A table, at least for the time being. It was not a given either, against a Genoa side who had won three in a row and four in five.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Both goals came in the first half as Alphadjo Cisse continued his excellent scoring form by firing in Adrien Rabiot’s cut-back. Christian Pulisic buried Goncalo Ramos’ square ball to double the advantage, and Ellertsson scored late to make it a tense finale, but Milan got over the line.

De Rossi was interviewed by DAZN (via MilanNews) after the full-time whistle at the Marassi to give his opinion on what was largely a positive performance from his side, but not the result to match.

Did you throw it away with individual mistakes?

“I congratulated my players. The performance was very good. I don’t like to talk about individual errors. I hear so many coaches talking about individual errors when they concede goals, not when they score. Kalulu also made an individual error on Baldanzi’s goal [against Juventus].

“That’s football. We can’t just rely on it when it suits us. There are ways to correct mistakes. We all made some mistakes collectively on the goals; we could have done better, but overall, I think the game was very good.”

It was an excellent reaction from the team despite going two behind…

“Before the last break, we were a bit more fragile, but my players always reacted. We were orderly in our reactions. Milan have a couple of players who hide the ball from you when it’s hot, but it never gets hot for them.

“They make you lose a bit of distance, and you have to put in a game that requires a lot of physical sacrifice. Everyone did it, and it makes me realise I have a team to be proud of. If we play like we did today, we’ll win more games than we’ll lose.”

“I shouldn’t judge him: he’s younger than me, but he has a lot more experience than me. I learn from him. We study him, we watch him. He plays with a system I really like.

“He puts a lot of quality on the wings, something I’ve also tried to do here. Certainly not Moreira’s level, but with the right proportions and possibilities.

“I like the way he communicate, he doesn’t blame his players even when they lose, he’s always honest when they lose. He’s doing an excellent job.”

“For anyone who loves this post, it’s exciting to see this player paint football like this at 41. Last season I had him man-marked, this season he played with two. He has more time on the ball.

“Maybe he goes a little slower, but the thought goes. We’ve clashed a couple of times and I told him, ‘I don’t play anymore so I can’t beat you’. But he’s beautiful, truly beautiful. He’s like Rabiot: world-class players.”
