Call of Duty: Warzone Mode Lets You Drop a Nuke for $1 Million, But There’s a Catch

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

Season 1 of Modern Warfare 4 saw the beginning of a new era for Call of Duty: Warzone with the debut of the Zodiac map. As a Resurgence map, Zodiac is compact and blends numerous combat environments for diverse and action-packed gameplay.

Despite Warzone’s full integration with Modern Warfare 4, battle royale modes feature unique mechanics, new equipment, and a fresh set of mastery camos to work towards.

This is DualShockers' review of COD: Modern Warfare 4, covering all the ins and outs of the campaign, multiplayer, and the new DMZ Extraction Mode.

Call of Duty players will know that earning a nuke is the ultimate display of dominance. Nukes are back in Warzone, but the way you acquire one is different from what you may be used to.

In previous iterations of Warzone, to drop a nuke, you would have to complete the Champion’s Quest. To unlock the Champion’s Quest, you needed to win five Warzone matches in a row or win 30 games within a season. Meeting either of those requirements would see the Champion’s Quest contract spawn at the beginning of your next match.

After accepting the Champion’s Quest, you and your squad would have to collect three radioactive elements from around the map that were needed to assemble the nuke at a bomb site. This would also mark you on the map for the entire lobby to see. Then, you’d have to defend the nuke, as other players could come in and defuse it. If the nuke was defused, your squad was wiped out, or the nuke timer hit zero before completing all the requirements, you’d fail to detonate the nuke.

It was by no means an easy feat, and it required a lot of teamwork to get the job done. With the launch of Warzone Season 1, you can still nuke the lobby, but some of the grind has been taken out of obtaining one.

To drop a nuke on the Warzone map, Zodiac, you must be the first player to purchase it from a Buy Station for $1 million cash. This means you and your squad must work together to loot as much cash as possible, earn cash from completing contracts, eliminating bots, and more, all while staying alive to keep it in your pocket until you reach the $1 million mark.

However, you can only detonate a nuke in Zodiac via Warzone’s Rally Point game mode. Rally Point is a training ground experience that supports a maximum of 20 players. It’s designed to allow you to explore the map, try out different weapons, and take your time mastering the mechanics. A nuke can’t be purchased from Buy Stations or acquired any other way in regular battle royale matches.

Anyone can load into a match of Rally Point and begin racking up cash to purchase a nuke, as there are no other requirements to unlock it.

When you gather enough cash to purchase the nuke, you and your squad will exfil from the battlefield while you watch the map get obliterated, and the match comes to an end.

You and your squad must work together to loot as much cash as possible, earn cash from completing contracts, eliminating bots, and more, all while staying alive to keep it in your pocket until you reach the $1 million mark.

For your efforts, you will earn the Perfect End weapon blueprint for the HAN 86 assault rifle, and you can show it off across Modern Warfare 4 and Warzone multiplayer modes.

At the time of writing, it’s unknown if a nuke will be added to standard battle royale modes. Those looking to secure the ultimate bragging rights will have to master Rally Point matches, for now.

Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 multiplayer has arrived, and it features skill-based matchmaking, with no Open Matchmaking playlist options in sight.

Trevor Noah on AI

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

by Oren Etzioni on Nov 8, 2026 at 9:52 amNovember 8, 2026 at 10:44 am

On Oct. 19, Trevor Noah launched Luminaries, a science conversation series at Boston’s Museum of Science. I joined him onstage, and Rana el Kaliouby moderated. Rana is a pioneer of emotion AI, software that reads human emotion, and the host of the Pioneers of AI podcast. She wrote afterward that she expected Trevor to be witty and funny, but “didn’t expect to be so blown away by his humanity and depth of ideas.”

Here are a few of his insights with his own words highlighted.

When an AI matchmaker Rana built in ChatGPT picked Trevor as her match, he pointed out that it knew very little about either of them. It assembled a plausible answer from what’s on the internet.

Trevor added that when you tell AI it made a mistake, it thanks you but often fails to correct the error: “It’s like a friend who’s been to a lot of therapy but doesn’t actually change.”

When asked how we protect ourselves from AI’s downsides, Trevor said the answer “goes back to something that is very un-technological and very un-AI.” He cited Robert Putnam’s book Bowling Alone, noting that places where communities form accrue many surprising benefits including more accountable governments.

I pushed back that communitarianism, the 1990s movement my father helped found to foster communities, had failed. Trevor responded that we hand too many ideas to intellectuals, who then “apply too much academia” to them. Communitarianism failed, he deadpanned, “because it’s called communitarianism.” An overly academic name.

He noted that nobody calls a phone a failure when the first version doesn’t sell; companies keep re-releasing it. He vowed never to stop investing in community.

Despite his concerns, Trevor has “yet to see a technology that blew up and then left everyone behind.” Cars, computers and the internet all spread to ordinary people.

AI has reached what he calls “work intelligence”: it can do the jobs we invented, but we keep inventing new ones. Salt and ice used to be precious. Now restaurants hand them out for free. What we value changes, and in Trevor’s view, we’re expendable only if we define being human by the tasks we value today.

Our conversation turned to AI’s impact on jobs.

Touring as a comic, Trevor saw towns hollowed out after Kodak and the automaker Studebaker left. Factory workers were told automation was progress. “White-collar workers are now experiencing what many blue-collar workers experienced for decades,” he said. The difference, he argued, is that white-collar workers are closer to power, so newspapers are covering it.

Trevor is confident new jobs will come, as they did after telephone exchange operators disappeared. What worries him is the gap. His analogy is the highway, where the following distance is the space that lets you stop before you hit the car ahead:

Trevor tells tech leaders that “it’s easier to be patient when you are in a position of privilege,” and added, “But ironically, they don’t have patience when it comes to quarterly earnings.”

After ChatGPT took off, show hosts kept telling him weak jokes that ChatGPT had written in a pale imitation of Trevor’s style. He rattled off four reasons AI struggles with humor: there isn’t enough comedy data, the model is a frozen snapshot while what society finds acceptable keeps shifting, comedy depends on knowing your audience (there are jokes you know are funny but you wouldn’t tell your mom), and companies have made models risk averse.

In addition to being the former host of The Daily Show, an award-winning author and a sought-after comedian, Trevor Noah is Microsoft’s Chief Questions Officer.

I reminded him of Picasso’s famous verdict on computers, “They are useless. They can only give you answers,” and joked that as Chief Questions Officer he has something rare these days: job security. He chuckled. Of course, his real job security comes from his depth and humanity, which feed his exceptional humor and wit.

Rana noted that everybody’s getting replaced by AI, but Trevor had played an AI, voicing Griot in Black Panther: Wakanda Forever.

In just a few weeks, AWS re:Invent brings advanced and expert-level sessions on the services you build on every day, from serverless and containers to databases and developer tools. Go hands-on with new launches the same week they’re announced, and hear directly from the engineers who built them. Experience the depth you won’t find anywhere else.

Collision of two trains in Egypt kills at least seven

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

President orders prompt investigation after one train derails in collision in al-Ayyat about 50km from Cairo

Seven people were killed and at least 125 injured when two trains collided on Sunday south of the Egyptian capital, Cairo, officials said.

The crash occurred in al-Ayyat district in Giza province, about 50km (30 miles) south of Cairo, when a train travelling to Fayoum province crashed into the rear of a train heading to the southern province of Aswan, according to the transport ministry.

An inspection indicated that the Cairo-Aswan train had been stationary on the same track, officials said.

The health ministry gave the casualty toll, adding more than 70 of the injured were discharged after receiving treatment.

Train derailments and crashes are common in Egypt, where the railway system has a history of mismanagement and poor maintenance of equipment. In recent years the government has announced modernisation initiatives to improve its railways.

Ayman Gamal, a resident of Kafr Girza in al-Ayyat where the crash occurred, said people rushed to the scene, pulled out survivors and called ambulances.

Gamal said he saw about 80 of the injured being taken by ambulances to hospital. Some passengers were trapped inside one of the carriages of the stationary train and died, he said.

Egypt’s president, Abdel Fatah al-Sisi, said in a social media post that he had ordered a prompt investigation.

Outside al-Wasta central hospital in Beni Suef province, where some of the injured were taken for treatment, survivors said passengers were in shock and struggling to breathe because of all the dust that was kicked up. Nearby villagers eventually got them out, they said.

Farha Gamal, a survivor who was inside the Fayyoum train, said she saw some of the passengers who appeared dead and others were struggling to find their way out of the car. “There were other cars that were completely mangled,” she said in an interview.

Emergency teams and cranes were dispatched to the crash site to clear the wreckage and restore train services to their normal schedule as soon as possible, the Egyptian railway authority said.

Last September, a train driver and his assistant were injured after two freight trains collided in the same district.

Egypt’s deadliest train crash was in 2002 when more than 300 people were killed after a fire broke out on an overnight train journeying from Cairo to southern Egypt.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Xabi Alonso was not looking for excuses after his Chelsea side was beaten 1-0 at Sunderland, admitting they did not defend or attack well enough.

The Blues returned to London with nothing, though, with an Enzo Le Fee penalty the difference between the sides.

Chelsea goalkeeper Mike Penders hit out at the decision, telling Sky Sports: ‘For me it wasn’t a penalty. He barely touched him, I think it was the other way round. He grabbed him.’

Alonso was not singing from the same hymn sheet, telling the BBC: ‘I haven’t seen it back. Nothing to comment on the referee. It is something that is our fault. We could have done better.’

He added: ‘I think it’s an action where he can defend better. It’s our mistake.’

Alonso was impressed by Brobbey over the game, not just in winning the penalty, but felt his defenders could have done better against the big Dutchman.

‘From transitions out of almost nothing he got a couple of chances, he got the penalty as well,’ he said. ‘But we have been preparing for that, we have tried, we could have done better.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘He is not unstoppable. He is stoppable but when you defend better than we did today.’

Clearly the former Real Madrid boss did not like he saw from his defenders and he was also frustrated with what his forwards produced.

‘I think we didn’t deserve more than probably one point,’ he said. ‘Not much happened, to be honest. We didn’t produce enough to deserve to win.

‘We were against a low block we missed a bit of patience, a bit of quality, a bit of creativity on the last third to create more chances, you need to be more consistent to move the ball from one side to the other that we didn’t do good enough.’

It was suggested to Alonso that Sunderland’s physical superiority meant they could bully Chelsea, but the manager said: ‘Not bullied. There’s situations that we could have defended better. Situations that we could have had better advantages in attack.’

Defeat left Chelsea fifth in the table, while Sunderland climbed to 18th.

Ocarina of Time remake datamine suggests Master Quest is on the way

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Ocarina of Time's notoriously tricky Master Quest mode could be making a comeback in the new remake.

Dataminer MrCheeze shared a list of strings from the remake's files on Bluesky, several of which tie the tag "MQ" to dungeons like Dodongo's Cavern.

In a follow-up, as spotted by TheGamer, they pointed to further entries, including "HasMQ", "IsMQModeExist", "NewGameMQ", and "ReleaseMQ".

Fellow dataminer OatmealDome added on Twitter/X that the mode will be unlocked by owning DLC, rather than arriving in an update, though "the data is incomplete". "I can confirm that the world is mirrored and dungeons are modified (e.g. cows in Jabu-Jabu's Belly)," they said.

If you've not played it, Master Quest is a remixed version of Ocarina of Time with rearranged dungeons and tougher puzzles. It first appeared as a bonus disc on GameCube, then came back in 2011's Ocarina of Time 3D, which unlocked it after you finished the main game. That version also mirrored the whole world, which matches what OatmealDome has found, and had enemies deal double damage.

[Ocarina of Time] #ootspoilersThere is data in the game files for Master Quest. It will be unlocked by *owning DLC* - it is not just an update.The data is incomplete, but I can confirm that the world is mirrored and dungeons are modified (e.g. cows in Jabu-Jabu's Belly).

As with any datamine, there's no guarantee it'll ever see the light of day. Nintendo hasn't announced any DLC for the remake, and it's not clear whether Master Quest would be free, like it was on 3DS, or paid.

The Ocarina of Time remake launched on Switch 2 on 5th November. Donlan called the new version an "astonishing remake" in his five-star Eurogamer Ocarina of Time review.

One BlackRock fund dominates Bitcoin’s billion-dollar ETF exodus

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Fidelity finished positive in the November 2-6 week, but the other funds collectively remained negative without IBIT.

BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF (IBIT) accounted for 94% of US spot Bitcoin ETFs' net withdrawals in the completed November 2-6 week, leaving one fund responsible for most of the category's retreat. That leaves the next recovery test focused on one fund: can IBIT’s withdrawals slow?

The 12 funds tracked by Farside Investors recorded weekly net outflows of $1,282 million. IBIT recorded net outflows of $1,210 million. Fidelity's Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) moved in the opposite direction, recording weekly net inflows of $93 million.

IBIT's share was 94.4% of the aggregate net withdrawal, after positive flows elsewhere offset part of the withdrawals. The denominator is net fund flows across the group, not gross redemptions or all Bitcoin selling.

All funds other than IBIT collectively recorded net outflows of $72 million during the week. Removing IBIT's withdrawals from that calculation would substantially reduce the shortfall while leaving the remaining group negative.

The daily pattern also matters. IBIT recorded withdrawals in each of the five sessions, while the group finished positive only on November 4. US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded net outflows of $412 million on November 6.

Fidelity was a counterweight across the week, despite recording withdrawals on Thursday. Three other funds also finished the week positive. The picture was therefore more uneven than a universal exit from Bitcoin funds, although the positive products did not offset the category's largest withdrawal source.

Wednesday's Fidelity-led inflows failed to persist: Thursday's withdrawals restored the negative aggregate reading, and Friday ended the week with another outflow.

iShares reported IBIT net assets of about $61 billion as of November 6. The week's withdrawals were roughly 2% of that ending asset base.

Redemptions also need care when connecting fund flows to Bitcoin selling. The IBIT prospectus describes both cash and in-kind settlement. A cash redemption involves the trust selling bitcoin and delivering cash. An in-kind redemption delivers bitcoin to an authorized participant, the intermediary handling the redemption, or its designated agent or client.

The prospectus permits two settlement routes. Shares are surrendered in both; the asset delivered back depends on the route.

Bitcoin is sold for cash, which is delivered to the authorized participant.

Bitcoin is delivered to the authorized participant or its designated agent or client.

Cash settlement sells bitcoin for cash; in-kind settlement delivers bitcoin.

Farside's net-flow figures do not disclose which route funded the week's withdrawals or what recipients subsequently did with bitcoin. They cannot establish an equivalent amount of contemporaneous open-market sales, investors' motives, or a proprietary bearish allocation by BlackRock.

Bitcoin's price was about $75,759 at 10:44 UTC on November 8, according to CryptoSlate's Bitcoin data.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics schedules the October Consumer Price Index (CPI) release for Tuesday, November 10, at 8:30 a.m. Eastern, or 13:30 UTC. Monday's completed fund session will provide another observation before the release; subsequent sessions will provide the comparison afterward.

The Federal Reserve explains that policy rates influence borrowing costs and broader financial conditions. Whether CPI changes policy expectations in a way that supports Bitcoin remains a conditional test.

For ETF demand, the distinction is observable. Smaller IBIT withdrawals alongside a negative aggregate total would show easing redemption pressure at IBIT. Positive aggregate flows would establish net ETF inflows for that session; participation across funds and persistence over subsequent completed sessions would provide stronger evidence of sustained fund demand.

Barnsley questioned for ‘really odd’ ticket decision v Sheffield Wednesday

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

This article is part of Football League World's 'Terrace Talk' series, which provides personal opinions from our FLW Fan Pundits regarding the latest breaking news, teams, players, managers, potential signings and more…

By the time Barnsley host Sheffield Wednesday on the 20th November, the Tykes will be hopeful that they enter the Yorkshire derby with some added confidence.

The Tykes have had a torrid start to the season under Daniel Stendel, sitting joint-bottom of the third tier standings, taking just nine points from their opening 12 league games.

Bromley are the only side below Barnsley by having a 10-worst goal difference, so results need to pick up quickly if they are to avoid the dreaded League Two trapdoor.

The Reds ended a six-game straight losing run by drawing 1-1 with promotion contenders Oxford United last weekend, and before their league clash against the Owls, host Aldershot Town in the first round of the FA Cup, while tthe following midweek sees them return to Oakwell to face Oldham Athletic in the EFL Trophy.

In an ideal scenario, Barnsley will be hoping to earn back-to-back victories, which would then stand them in great stead for what was expected to be a raucous Oakwell against Wednesday.

However, there are fears now that Oakwell may not be as full and lively due to the club's ticket prices.

Barnsley v Sheffield Wednesday is the Friday night football on the 20th November, with Oakwell hosting a Yorkshire derby.

But there’s been criticism aimed at Barnsley for their ticket pricing for the fixture.

It was announced in the last few days that the Tykes are charging £35 for a standard adult ticket against the Owls, with some viewing that as extortionate, given the game is being broadcast live on Sky Sports.

Barnsley's ticket prices for the visit of Sheffield Wednesday have NOT gone down well...

Along with the adult ticket pricing, seniors (65+) are charged at £25, young adults (19-21) are also set at £25, while under-19s are £16 and under-14s are set at £10.

With this in mind, FLW’s Barnsley fan pundit has given his thoughts over the decision to charge adults £35 a ticket.

Speaking to Football League World, Barnsley fan pundit Andy Symcox has questioned the Tykes about their decision to charge adults £35 a ticket for their meeting with Sheffield Wednesday, believing the pricing is ‘really odd’ and will keep some fans away from Oakwell for such an important clash.

Andy told FLW: “For me, the decision to charge £35 for a matchday ticket at Barnsley versus Sheffield Wednesday, on the surface, seems really odd.

“The game itself is on Sky, and thus, how many fans will decide not to go to Oakwell to watch it on their TV’s instead, quoting the excessive price.

Barnsley fans, do you agree with our fan pundit's thoughts? What do you think of the adult ticket price against Sheffield Wednesday? Comment below!

“However, I wonder whether or not this is part of the reciprocal arrangement, as Sheffield Wednesday have notoriously charged very high prices in the past for games at Hillsborough, so much so that I personally refuse to go there due to the very high cost of tickets.

“On balance, however, given that it is on Sky, I find it a poor decision.”

A massive problem with football throughout the country is pricing, with tickets getting more and more unaffordable as the cost of living gets tougher and tougher.

And Barnsley are offering little help to their supporters through their latest price sting, with some supporters simply unable to pay such prices for a League One standard football match.

In what should have been a fiery cauldron of noise given it’s a local derby, Barnsley will see fewer supporters at Oakwell on that Friday evening, with some electing to watch it on TV given it’s being broadcast by Sky Sports.

Because of this, Barnsley are driving their supporters away from a game that has the potential to turn their season right around, and they will only have themselves to blame come that night if Oakwell has a soulless atmosphere and contributes towards yet another negative performance and result.

In what is such a tough situation for the club, they need every single one of their supporters to be right behind the team and creating an atmosphere that makes Oakwell a fortress again, but the club are currently killing any chance of that by charging supporters far too much money.

It reinforces the sad state that football is in at the minute, with clubs failing in their duties to make football an affordable escape.

8 SNES JRPGs That Reward Players for Exploring the World More Than Grinding Levels

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Murillo Zerbinatto is a JRPG enthusiast, writer, and editor. He has played over 300 RPGs during his gaming career, with 90% of them being JRPGs.

When the JRPG genre was born, its progression was as basic as it gets: kill enemies, gain levels, become stronger. The in-game economy also revolved around earning gold to buy new equipment, but leveling up was the sole driver of power. Many NES and SNES JRPGs demanded some grinding, and since most battles were random encounters, you naturally did it as you moved along.

From classic Final Fantasy to Undertale, these 10 JRPGs reward understanding your enemy more than stacking up levels

Occasionally, however, games emerged that promoted more horizontal progression, focusing less on experience points or level-ups and instead rewarding players who explored every nook and cranny of the world or engaged deeply with unique mechanics introduced in each title. Today, I will explore several SNES JRPGs that reward players for venturing out into the world or leveraging a game's unique feature rather than mindlessly grinding for levels.

No better way to kick things off than with a JRPG that doesn't even feature traditional levels. Illusion of Gaia is part of what is commonly called the Quintet Trilogy, a set of action RPGs crafted with tremendous care, each bringing its own distinct flavor. In Illusion of Gaia, we control Will, who can transform into two other distinct forms at various stages of the adventure: Freedan or Shadow.

To grow stronger, you need to clear out every enemy in a given room, which grants a jewel that permanently increases the protagonist's core attributes. It's a small bit of grind, but instead of defeating monsters repeatedly, you only need to defeat each enemy once per zone. It feels far more gratifying and rewarding because you maintain a clean, consistent sense of progression whenever you choose to clear rooms.

Star Ocean never received an official Western release on the SNES. Yet, it already featured several mechanics that would become defining staples for the franchise, especially if you look at the remake Star Ocean: First Departure. In tri-Ace's JRPG, gaining levels helps because it grants SP to upgrade crafting skills, letting you synthesize equipment far more powerful than anything you can find in shops. In a sense, this rewards players for exploring system mechanics rather than mindlessly slaying mobs.

The JRPG also introduced the Private Action system to build deeper connections with party members, alongside optional character recruitments and a dynamic relationship mechanic. The more you engage with everything the world of Star Ocean has to offer, the greater your rewards will be.

Terranigma is another standout title from the Quintet Trilogy, but unlike Illusion of Gaia, it actually features a traditional leveling system. Defeating enemies yields experience points, which raise attributes on level-up. Still, because Terranigma is an action RPG, execution and player skill matter far more than raw stats, so you never need to grind endlessly just to advance through the story.

Enemies occasionally drop gems you can use to buy new equipment, items, and spells, which is where the magic of exploration takes center stage. You can convert every Magirock you discover into a spell, with more options unlocking as you progress. After casting a spell, the Magirock reverts to its base form, requiring you to slot another spell into it, which directly rewards those who seek out hidden items. On top of that, the town-building sidequests form the emotional heart of Terranigma's narrative, uncovering their full potential only for players willing to track them down.

Breath of Fire is my favorite JRPG series, and each new entry has only refined its craft. While Breath of Fire II on the SNES requires a bit of early farming, especially during the Colosseum events and when Randy joins the party, a specific mechanic will empower your party members far more than any raw level-up: the Shaman system.

After you build your own village and advance through the story, Ryu can recruit Shamans hidden across the world. Using their power, you can fuse one or more Shamans with a party member, altering their form and making them exponentially stronger. For instance, Spar, who feels somewhat underwhelming by default, transforms into an absolute powerhouse through Shaman fusion, with similar massive power spikes applying to Katt and Sten. While levels remain relevant, tracking down Shamans and executing the correct fusions provides a far greater boost to overall combat strength.

The old school villains really were some of the best, as these SNES JRPGs demonstrate.

Character progression in Final Fantasy VI works completely differently depending on who is in your active party. Certain characters like Cyan, Sabin, and Terra acquire new skills and spells simply by leveling up, but others like Edgar, Gau, and Strago require you to explore the world, purchase specialized items, and battle rare monsters. Also, if you want to min-max your stats, you'll want to hold off on leveling until you acquire Espers.

Leveling up in Final Fantasy VI only increases base HP and MP until you unlock Espers. Once acquired, each Esper grants specific attribute bonuses when you level up, letting you tailor and optimize character builds. While min-maxing is not strictly necessary for a casual playthrough, it adds a fascinating layer of depth. Additionally, during the second half of the game in the World of Ruin, the entire party-gathering mechanic hinges on world exploration if you want to re-recruit your full roster.

One of the most common praises directed at Lufia II centers on its brilliant dungeon design. The game features unique puzzle gimmicks and exploration mechanics reminiscent of The Legend of Zelda, utilizing specialized tools to solve environmental hazards. Every dungeon comes packed with secret rooms containing rare loot. It even carries a touch of Metroidvania flair, as tools acquired in later dungeons can be taken back to earlier areas to uncover hidden rewards.

Dragon Eggs are also scattered across the world and yield stat-enhancing potions, rare fruits, or powerful rings once you deliver them. Most importantly, Lufia II features Capsule Monsters that battle alongside your party. They act independently and evolve by eating items from your inventory, meaning you need a vast hoard of gear to feed your companions. Raising them isn't about grinding levels but about exploring dungeons, accumulating gold, buying items, and tracking down rare drops.

If a JRPG strikes a clean balance between combat progression and world exploration, it is Super Mario RPG. Mindless grinding is rarely necessary. And while leveling up offers significant stat boosts, grinding levels can feel overly tedious. True to its Mario roots, the game packs plenty of environmental puzzles and secrets that make exploration well worth your jumping time.

For example, expanding your maximum Flower Points requires hunting down specific hidden items across the world. You can also hunt for Frog Coins, a rare currency you can trade for powerful accessories and items. Game-changing equipment also hides off the beaten path, such as the Lazy Shell, which bestows absurd defensive stats that practically trivialize combat. It's a prime example of how vital exploration can be in shaping your party's strength.

Among the endless stream of compliments Chrono Trigger has received over the years, praise for its pacing remains one of the most common. Chrono Trigger omitted random encounters entirely, an unheard-of design choice for its era. Furthermore, the game never demanded grinding, letting you push through main-story challenges smoothly without ever looking back. That holds true right up until you acquire the Epoch.

Once you gain access to the Epoch, you can travel between eras seamlessly to visit hidden islands packed with optional dungeons and formidable bosses. These optional challenges present a far steeper curve than the main narrative, but with proper preparation and strategy, they are completely manageable. These side areas contain the absolute best endgame equipment while offering deep narrative payoffs for the party's backstories. On top of that, the game features numerous alternate endings accessible via New Game+, letting you confront Lavos at different points in time and proving that Chrono Trigger's spirit of exploration extends directly into its narrative structure.

These SNES JRPGs have stories Where the World Feels Like It’s Slowly Falling Apart Around the Player.

Which Airlines Fly To The Most Destinations? Some May Surprise You

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Recently, I wrote a post about the airlines that fly to the most countries. Turkish Airlines wins that title by a long shot, and has a 30+ country lead over the second most “global” airline.

In this post, I want to address a different but related topic — which airlines serve the most destinations? As you’d expect, this is a very different list. That’s because serving a lot of countries is much easier if you’re an airline that has a lot of service in Europe and Africa, given the number of countries there that are in close proximity. It’s one of the reason that US airlines don’t score that well when it comes to absolute number of countries served, despite otherwise being among the biggest carriers in the world.

However, the list of total number of destinations served is a different story…

I don’t think anyone will be surprised by the three airlines that serve the most destinations. As it currently stands:

This makes sense. In terms of fleets, the “big three” US carriers are among the biggest in the world. A majority of their networks are domestic, which is why the airlines don’t rank that high when it comes to total number of countries served, but they do great when it comes to the total number of destinations served. Note that the number of destinations also includes flights operated by their regional subsidiaries.

I don’t think the order above will surprise anyone. United is of course the most global US airline, and has a very impressive route network, including with ultra long haul flights. Meanwhile American is a massive domestic airline, and has a strong network in Latin America and the Caribbean, which is why it doesn’t appear that far behind United in terms of international destinations. And Delta, ummm, gets a revenue premium.

Just in the interest of being thorough, I’m sure some people are curious how many destinations Southwest serves, since it’s an absolutely massive airline in the United States, though doesn’t operate long haul flights. That number is less impressive — it’s a total of 120 destinations, including 104 domestic destinations and 16 international destinations. That’s because the airline is more about depth of service than breadth of service, and doesn’t have a regional subsidiary.

After US carriers, which airlines otherwise top the list of most destinations served? Most people wouldn’t be surprised to learn that Turkish takes that spot, serving 269 destinations, including 46 domestic destinations and 223 international destinations.

What about after that? Here are the next eight airlines (I think this is where some of the airlines may surprise people):

It’s interesting just how different the airlines on the list are. You have the “big three” Chinese carriers, the “big three” European carriers, and then you have Ryanair and Air Canada.

When you put it all together, below is the chart with the 12 airlines serving the most total destinations.

In the interest of being thorough, for those curious about Gulf carriers, Qatar Airways is in first place among them, serving 175 destinations, so it doesn’t quite make the list. Meanwhile Ethiopian Airlines, as Africa’s most global airline, serves 163 destinations

While Turkish Airlines flies to the most countries of any airline in the world, it doesn’t serve the most destinations. Instead that title goes to United, with the “big three” US carriers taking the three top spots. Then Turkish is in fourth place, and is followed by the “big three” Chinese carriers, the “big three” European carriers, and then also Ryanair and Air Canada.

Are you surprised by any of the airlines serving the most destinations?

Hearts unbeaten home record obliterated as swashbuckling Falkirk earn 31-year first

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Rampant Falkirk obliterated Hearts’ 18-month unbeaten home record in the Scottish Premiership with a stunning 5-2 win at Tynecastle as they leapfrogged the Jambos into third place.

Wouter Vrancken’s beleaguered side, missing 11 players through injury amid a gruelling fixture list, were in disarray as the swashbuckling Bairns romped into a five-goal lead early in the second half through Finley Barbrook, Owen Eames, Liam Henderson and Jacob Murrell’s double.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Hearts hit back through substitute Sabri Guendouz and a Claudio Braga penalty, but Falkirk were well worthy of making it five top-flight wins in a row for the first time since 1995.

It was a first league defeat at Tynecastle for Hearts since April 2025. To exacerbate their grim predicament, the Jambos, who have won just two of their last 11 matches, lost yet another senior player as Malachi Fagan-Walcott limped off in the second half.

There were two changes to the Hearts side that started Thursday’s Conference League defeat to Thun as Stephen Kingsley and Guendouz were replaced by Yan Dhanda and Landry Kabore.

Falkirk boss John McGlynn, named manager of the month for October, made one enforced change from the side that defeated Celtic last Sunday as Jack McMillan took over from injured right-back Ryan Strain.

The Bairns seized the lead in the fourth minute. After Murrell’s close-range effort from Scott Tanser’s cross was brilliantly saved by Beau Reus, Barbrook reacted quickest to drill home a low, angled finish from eight yards out.

Hearts threatened an equaliser in the 10th minute, but Kabore’s effort on the turn from the edge of the six-yard box was superbly blocked by Scott Bain.

Falkirk doubled their lead in the 17th minute after the hosts were caught wide open on the counter-attack. Dhanda’s shot from distance was charged down, allowing the Bairns to break swiftly, with Brad Spencer weighting his pass perfectly for Eames, who fired past the exposed Reus.

Braga thought he had pulled one back for Hearts in the 20th minute, but it was ruled out for handball.

Falkirk took full advantage of this reprieve by adding a third in the 33rd minute as Murrell peeled off Fagan-Walcott at the back post to nod home Deniss Melniks’ cross from the right.

The visitors struck again in first-half stoppage-time as Henderson curled high past Reus after Tanser’s corner broke to him just inside the box.

As jeers rained down from an irate home support, Vrancken replaced midfielders Laurent Mendy and Dhanda with Guendouz and Matthew Gillies for the start of the second half.

But within two minutes of the restart Falkirk notched again as Murrell fired home after being set up by Ethan Ross.

Hearts got off the mark when Guendouz drilled in his first goal for the club from just inside the box in the 50th minute.

Braga then tucked away a penalty in the 74th minute after Guendouz was tripped by Lewis Neilson, but it was not enough to prevent a third straight defeat for the Jambos.

2027 Kawasaki Jet Skis deliver a supercharged, eye-opening performance

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 8, 2026 5:21 PM EST

In 1964, then-Pontiac division president John Z. Delorean (yes, that Delorean, of Back to the Future movie car fame) invented the muscle car category by bolting the big-block V8 engine from the company’s full-size cars into its lighter intermediate-sized chassis to create the legendary Pontiac GTO.

Kawasaki has done the same thing for its Jet Skis for the 2027 model year by installing the 310-horsepower supercharged 1.5-liter engine from its bigger Ultra models into the sporty, agile Jet Ski STX hull. The result is a truly eye-opening performance with what the company says is its quickest Jet Ski yet.

Kawasaki established the personal watercraft industry with the 1973 introduction of the 27-horsepower JS400 Jet Ski. Since that time, however, the one-time industry leader has slipped in popularity compared to its rivals in the market.

The company’s 2027 plan to recapture customers includes an embrace of high performance. Kawasaki has rolled out five new models for 2027, but the stars of the show are the high-performance Jet Skis meant to excite customers.

The original Jet Ski employed a smokey 400 cc 2-stroke engine that grew to 800 cc over time. Those early Jet Skis were tricky stand-up models that delivered responsive handling but presented a learning curve that saw newcomers falling off repeatedly as they learned to master the machine.

The solution was the introduction of the more stable sit-down models that traded responsiveness for stability. It was a trade that so many riders were willing to make that eventually Kawasaki phased out the stand-up models entirely.

The industry shifted to cleaner 4-stroke engines in the ‘90s, and 1999 saw the arrival of the 1.5-liter engine based on Kawasaki’s sport motorcycle powerplants in its Jet Skis, packing 150 horsepower. In 2007, the engineering team bolted a supercharger to the same engine, producing 250 horsepower.

Now, thanks to the technology of Eaton’s Twin Vortices System supercharger design (which debuted on the 2009 Chevrolet Corvette ZR1), power has reached 310 horsepower. Packed into the agile STX sit-down hull to create the STX 310 model, the big motor lets riders rip across the water at 65 mph.

This drivetrain employs a 4-into-2-into-1 exhaust with dual water mufflers to minimize backpressure, maximizing the engine’s power and efficiency. The 160-mm jet pump has a three-blade impeller whose blade angles are optimized for peak acceleration and thrust.

Kawasaki let us ride the top-of-the-line STX 310RX, which lists for $20,199. There is a slightly less-well-equipped STX 310R that goes just as fast for $18,799. The test unit featured a 7-inch display panel containing the speedometer, tachometer, supercharger boost level, fuel level, trim setting, power mode setting, and compass. The rear-view camera on the STX lets the rider keep an eye on skiers, wakeboarders, or tube riders behind the watercraft.

We’ve gotten familiar with electronically controlled modes in modern cars, trucks, and motorcycles, and they are also present here in the Jet Ski STX 310RX. They are Smart Learning Operation (SLO), Lower Power Operation (LPO), Middle Power Operation (MPO), and Full Power Operation (FPO).

I started riding in MPO, thinking that would be a good get-acquainted scenario and I was pleasantly surprised at the STX 310’s speed. I guess I shouldn’t have been, considering that it has 310 horsepower. After a bit of that, I switched to FPO, and found the throttle response much touchier and acceleration quicker–perfect for big fun.

MPO limits peak power to 80 percent, while FPO unleashes all 310 horses. LPO makes the throttle response even more sluggish and further restricts maximum power to 60 percent. SLO mode requires a special yellow key and is aimed at true beginners, who, realistically, probably should be learning on something other than this supercharged beast.

Other gadgets include the Launch Control system, which automatically adjusts the jet pump’s trim over the course of an acceleration run, keeping it optimized as the boat’s attitude changes during the run. There’s also a handy one-touch 5-mph cruise control, good for complying with no-wake zones.

Hot-dogging on the STX is a good time, thanks to a hull design that was created for corner carving, without much risk of pitching the rider into orbit mid-corner. There are also some nice practical features, like a Neutral that preserves some degree of steering capability.

Jet Skis have no rudder, relying on thrust from the engine to steer them, so when the throttle is released, or the boat is shifted to neutral, you’d expect it to be adrift. But Kawasaki employs a thrust reverser that lets the STX simultaneously have no forward thrust while also having some steering capacity. That reverser also provides for a Reverse position that lets the ride back the STX.

Reverse is one of the amenities that led to the demise of original-style stand-up Jet Skis, which aren’t suited to such features. Kawasaki tried briefly to revive the format in 2017, but says that its new stand-up 2027 models are Back to the Future to stay this time.

I was pleasantly surprised to find that long-dormant stand-up riding skills were still sufficient for me to take off on the first try without falling.

Kawasaki offers two versions of its classic stand-up model for 2027, the Jet Ski SX-R 160 and SX-X 160. These are powered by 160-hp naturally aspirated versions of the 1.5-liter engine, with the difference between them being that the SX-R has a traditional-style moveable handlebar that starts off lying flat and the rider lifts it as they stand.

The SX-X has a fixed motorcross-style handlebar, giving riders a choice between the two styles. The test model was the SX-X motocrosser. I’d never ridden one like that before, but I found the fixed bar seemed to make it easier to pull myself up as the ski got underway.

However, after falling off, as one does on these things, you have to reach up from the water to reattach your wrist lanyard to the ignition interlock. This is the thing that shuts the ski off when you fall so that it doesn’t leave you behind, and you have to reconnect it to restart the machine.

But with the fixed bar up there pretty high from the water, I was happy to have my 35-inch sleeve length. Someone with more normal-length arms might find it hard to reach the switch while floating in the water alongside.

Just as I’d remembered stand-ups, the SX model is tremendously agile and fun for turns and jumps. And just as I remembered, it is easy to fall off in the process. Once it starts to roll onto its side, adding power might get you to ride out of it, or, as in my case, it might just make you hit the water while going even faster!

It doesn’t matter which handlebar style you prefer because both the SX-X 160 and SX-R 160 are $11,999. Getting one of these would allow you to cultivate some old-school riding skills while taking advantage of some throwback value. That was another key component of the original Pontiac GTO’s muscle car legend, so it is good to see in the Kawasaki showrooms for 2027.

French carmaker Renault wants to get into the military robot business

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Forget what your Fox News-addicted uncle told you about cheese-eating surrender monkeys. The French military has centuries of storied military victories and traditions behind it, and Renault wants in on that action with a new, autonomous electric vehicle platform designed to tackle the Armée de Terre’s most demanding missions.

Co-developed by the EV experts at Renault Group and the defense experts at Belgian electronics firm John Cockerill, the new Forefront concept previews a new, modular ground vehicle that can be rapidly industrialized at a controlled cost, with unique mobility and off-road performance, and capable of autonomously navigating complex terrain. The result is a machine that could front-line logistical capabilities for both defense and civilian applications, enabling missions to be carried out remotely in very high-risk and hard‑to‑reach environments.

“Forefront Concept … demonstrates the contribution that Renault Group’s industrial and technological expertise can make to a strategic sector such as unmanned ground vehicles with multiple applications expected across both civilian and military sectors in the years to come,” explains François Provost, CEO, Renault Group. “This innovative project brings together two complementary areas of expertise: Renault Group’s in the rapid, large-scale and cost-effective development and production of high-performance, reliable technological products and John Cockerill’s in the defense industry.”

Renault is quick to point out that its new concept isn’t just suitable for military work. Although it’s worth pointing out that the Renault-provided image, above, isn’t just AI slop: it would be impractical if it was real, with no real obvious way to get those RCP pipes on or off the thing. That said, presenting their military killbot as a construction mule is at least more credible than the “ambulance” and “troop transport” use cases we’ve seen touted by similar concepts on the American side.

Forefront seems to be little more than a rendering at this stage, with the first of the Forefront concept prototypes set to take part in a number of exercises and trials in the months ahead to further refine the concept.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

East Fife have appointed former Rangers midfielder Scott Arfield as their new player-manager.

The 38-year-old replaces Dick Campbell who was sacked by the League One club last month after a run of eight matches without a victory.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Arfield has been without a club since departing Livingston in May after taking on the role of caretaker player-manager for the final three matches of the 2025-26 season.

The move to Bayview represents a first full-time step into management for the former Canada international, who also had two spells with Falkirk as well as stints with Huddersfield Town, Burnley, Charlotte FC and Bolton Wanderers.

“I am absolutely delighted to be the new manager of East Fife and I cannot wait to get started,” Arfield, who has signed a contract until May 2029, told the club website.

“I feel I am ready to go into coaching after learning a lot from excellent managers during my playing career, and I am grateful to East Fife for believing in me and for giving me this opportunity.

“My aim is now to create opportunities for others and to build a team which will excite East Fife supporters and attract the attention of the local community. It is a highly respected club which has great potential, and it is a privilege to be given the chance to lead it forward.”

Liam Anderson, chairman of East Fife, added: “We are delighted to have agreed a deal with Scott to become our new player-manager.

“This an innovative appointment and gives a clear indication of our ambitions going forward. I am sure that the arrival of Scott will be welcomed by our fans who are keen to see an exciting brand of football at Bayview.”

Arfield, who played for Rangers in the Europa League final four years ago, will take charge of the Fifers for the first time when Peterhead visit Bayview on Saturday. He also hopes to resume playing this month once he regains match fitness.

An East Fife statement added: "The board of directors is grateful to the interim management team of Jim Moffat, Pat Slattery and Brian Easton, who have guided the squad through the recent period of vacancy. Jim will continue as goalkeeping coach, Pat will assist with coaching while injury prevents him from playing, and Brian returns to on-field duties."

At some point after the film’s release and rise to holiday favorite, as Richter told Obsessed: The Podcast, Elf producer Shauna Robertson received a phone call from an unlikely screenwriter pitching a meeting about Elf 2.

“Somebody reached out to her and said, ‘The Edge from U2 has written a sequel to Elf, and he wants to talk to you about it,’” Richter said. “It’s the weirdest story, but apparently it was fully written somewhere in the world. There is an Edge-penned Elf 2 out there.”

Richter added that the meeting “never happened.” “It’s one of those things where she’s like, ‘All right, sure, I’ll meet him,’ and then it just petered out and never happened,” Richter said. Viral retro gaming creator Jacob Payne dies as family warns fans of impersonators

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Viral retro gaming creator Jacob Payne, better known as Retro Jake, has died, his family announced on Instagram on November 7.

His family said on Instagram that Payne was “in heaven now” and had “fought until the very end,” while asking followers to keep his loved ones in their thoughts and prayers as they take time to mourn.

Payne’s death came after he had been largely absent from regular uploads. His last listed YouTube video, titled “Life Update. Long Video Incoming,” was posted on October 25, while a local report said his family had announced he was hospitalized with severe heart failure the following week. His family has not publicly provided a formal cause of death.

The Retro Jake account later issued a warning after the family said scammers were already attempting to exploit the situation. Followers were told not to send money or answer questions from anyone claiming to represent Payne, his relatives or his friends.

The family said all legitimate information would be shared through Retro Jake’s official Facebook page. It also warned that no financial support would be requested unless it was announced through that account.

Payne was known for buying, selling and trading retro games while showcasing collections and customer interactions at his stores. He also supported local causes, including a “Console For Kids” initiative with the Richmond Police Department that delivered video game systems to Girls Inc. last Christmas.

Binance resumes HYPE withdrawals, but how will altcoin navigate $77.6M in shorts?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid emerged as the most-viewed token in the last 24 hours, followed by Kinetiq [KNTQ], Bitcoin [BTC], and Pudgy Penguins [PENGU], respectively. At press time, the altcoin was trading at $77, with its daily trading volume hitting $442 million. What spurred this interest though?

Well, this spike in searches was fueled by some news from Binance. It announced the resumption of Hyperliquid [HYPE] and Aptos withdrawals following its late-October hack. It took the exchange 10 days to restart withdrawals.

Back when it was announced, the halt in services frustrated users despite Hyperliquid chain not being directly involved. Binance rectified the issue, citing that withdrawals had been paused to prevent assets from getting stuck in processing and/or loss of funds.

With services gradually resuming across the board, Binance has assured users they will continue to restore additional assets and networks in phases.

In fact, a couple of tokens have already been re-enabled, including altcoins and stablecoins on Ethereum [ETH] and Solana [SOL] networks. As far as HYPE is concerned, the timing here is interesting since the altcoin has been bearish since the crypto-exchange was hacked.

Institutions have been building their short positions, with that of Abraxas four times bigger than that of Wintermute. In fact, Abraxas’s perp exposure was in excess of $451 million, while Wintermute’s was about $107 million.

HYPE‘s $77.60 million short position was the second-largest for Abraxas, about half of Bitcoin’s at $158 million. The firm’s short positions were at an unrealized profit of more than $30.77 million.

Not only are some institutions selling, but so are smart traders. For instance, a whale sold 433K HYPE with a 10x leverage but seemed to be sitting on lifetime losses of about $20.92 million.

All these factors, together, have been piling on selling pressure on the token. In fact, this week’s candle eclipsed last week’s green candle that rallied from $76 and closed at $88.

At the time of writing, the altcoin was trading at around $75. This is a key support level that has been critical for hype since late August.

HYPE has fallen by over 12% in the last seven days, with the altcoin being among the worst altcoin performers in the market. The resumption of withdrawals on Binance could revive traders’ confidence and change broader crypto sentiment for the better.

However, if bears break below $75, HYPE could fall to $70 or $60, respectively.

Nowadays, tech is everywhere when we travel, from power banks and portable fans to smart suitcases that follow their owners around the airport. But many of these devices come with a hidden risk.

In October, a British Airways flight was forced to make an emergency landing after a battery fire burned one of the passengers — and this is hardly an isolated incident. The Federal Aviation Administration reported 28 battery fire events on passenger and cargo planes in the first quarter of 2026 alone. That’s on track to beat last year’s tally of 97 events, up from 89 in 2024.

Fires can be particularly dangerous in the enclosed space of an airplane, so it’s critical for passengers to do their part by packing batteries correctly. Here’s an overview of the types of batteries you need to watch out for, what devices they’re in and how to pack them safely and legally.

Any battery can start a fire if stored improperly, but the biggest threats are lithium metal (non-rechargeable) and lithium-ion (rechargeable) batteries. These batteries have exploded in popularity in recent years. Unfortunately, they are also at risk of, quite literally, exploding.

According to the FAA, “All lithium-ion batteries are capable of overheating and experiencing a process called thermal runaway.”

Thermal runaway is a self-sustaining chemical reaction that produces extreme heat (up to 1,500 degrees F within seconds), toxic smoke, ultrafine particles and flames. It can occur without warning due to various factors, including damage to the battery, overheating, overcharging, exposure to water and manufacturing defects.

Lithium battery fires are often resistant to traditional extinguishing agents, making them tough for cabin crews to tackle. And since planes recirculate air rather than drawing fresh air from outside, toxic fumes from lithium fires can pose a health hazard to passengers long after the flames are extinguished.

As a result, the aviation industry has been tightening the rules on lithium batteries.

The most common culprits behind aircraft fires are cell phones, laptops, vapes and power banks. For example, three passengers were injured when a laptop battery caught fire on an American Airlines plane in 2024, and a power bank caused a similar emergency on a Virgin Australia flight in 2025.

However, lots of other devices can contain lithium batteries, such as:

To determine whether your device has a lithium battery, check the label or manual. The chemical symbol “Li” generally indicates the presence of lithium; you may see terms such as "Li-polymer," "LiPo” or "Li+." For coin-cell batteries, the label will typically start with “CR” if lithium is involved.

As you can see, there’s a good reason for all the restrictions around batteries on planes — but they can be confusing. If you’ve ever wondered how you should store your camera battery or whether you can pack that portable fan in your checked luggage, you’ve come to the right place.

Here, I’ll answer all of your, ahem, burning questions about batteries.

The regulations around lithium batteries depend on the battery size and whether they are alone or installed in a device.

Non-rechargeable batteries are limited to 2 grams of lithium per battery, while rechargeable batteries are limited to a rating of 100 watt hours. According to TSA, this covers “nearly all types of lithium batteries used by the average person in their electronic devices.” Larger batteries require approval from the airline.

TSA prohibits spare or uninstalled lithium batteries — which include power banks and cell phone charging cases — in checked baggage. These batteries must go in your carryon, and if you end up gate-checking your bag, you must remove them and keep them with you during the flight.

For lithium batteries contained within a device, you can pack them in your checked bag as long as the device is fully powered off and “protected from accidental activation,” according to the FAA. For devices that generate extreme heat (such as lighters), you must protect the device from switching on inside your checked bag by removing the battery or heating element.

Your specific airline may have additional rules regarding lithium batteries. For instance, Southwest has one of the strictest battery policies in the sky: Passengers are limited to one power bank per person, batteries are prohibited in overhead bins and they must be visible while in use.

The above guidelines apply only to lithium batteries. “Dry” batteries, such as AAs, button cells and 9-volts, are allowed in both checked and carry-on bags.

However, FAA guidelines state that “Battery terminals (usually the ends) must be protected from short circuit (i.e., the terminals must not come in contact with other metal).” You can keep your batteries safe by storing them in their original packaging, a battery case, the battery pocket of a camera bag or a plastic baggy. However, the FAA does not recommend packing rechargeable batteries in their charging unit.

Carrying lithium batteries onboard with you won’t prevent them from catching fire, but it will make it easier for flight crews to respond to any incidents. You can do your part by following these guidelines:

Baggage regulations can be tricky, but it’s worth paying attention. Passengers who don’t follow the rules can be slapped with serious fines — and, more importantly, they pose a safety hazard to everyone on their flight.

Follow the packing tips above to travel safely and stress-free, and check out our other travel safety tips:

Barnsley FC and Daniel Stendel welcomed fun back like an old friend in FA Cup win v Aldershot Town - analysis

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

As Daniel Stendel sat down to dissect Barnsley’s 7-2 FA Cup win over Aldershot Town, he offered a wry smile.

He had been asked about the two goals his side leaked late in a game that had already been taken well beyond the opposition.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

It was a question he will have known was coming, and a question most managers would have enjoyed answering to demonstrate their forensic and demanding nature. The word ‘standards’ would have most likely been used.

Stendel, however, had no interest in poking holes in a mostly scintillating performance. And who could blame him?

Barnsley’s campaign has been largely devoid of fun and rife with simmering discontent. With Bonfire Night celebrations spilling over into the weekend, Stendel clearly felt the Reds needed to simply enjoy the sparks.

“We keep the positive things,” said Stendel. “It's normal that I think about the two goals, but I think we analysed a lot in the last couple of weeks about some situations, some games, and why we didn't win the game in the end.

“I think today is time to say ‘good result, we're in the hat for the next draw’.”

Barnsley flew out of the traps at a dizzying pace and the brakes were simply never applied. By half-time, Cameron McGeehan and Vimal Yoganathan braces and a Reyes Cleary effort had Aldershot dead and buried.

McGeehan completed his hat-trick after the break and Rylan Brownlie got in the act, with the latter rounding off the afternoon after Aldershot had twice hit back via Annisuis Lewis.

“It was good to see that we used the spaces that we get, and a goal after a set-piece.

“I think three goals after crosses was good, especially that. It's one thing that we try to change and what we train in training, why we said we have to cross more balls in the box. And yeah, it was good today.”

Toxicity has enveloped Oakwell in recent months and while it has not been Stendel’s doing, it is clear he is relishing the trickling return of some positivity.

His side’s hard-fought draw with Oxford United a week prior evoked nostalgia, memories of a first spell in which supporter connection was strong.

They did not require the same spirit against the Shots but bluntly, it was just nice to see some goalscoring fun. It is a pleasure with which the Oakwell faithful have not been well-acquainted of late.

“We needed this game,” said Stendel. “It's a good opportunity for everybody to prove what we want to do next week and to get some confidence in defence and offence for winning duels, try to do things, cross the ball early, and in the box you can score goals.

“We have tough weekends in the past, and I think it was good for everybody, for the mindset, for the training time next week, for the game, especially before the weekend game.”

Barnsley will host Oldham Athletic in the Jersey Mike’s Trophy on Wednesday (November 11) but it is clear Stendel’s primary focus is on the Brick Community Stadium. That is where Wigan Athletic will entertain the Reds in a battle of two of League One’s bottom four sides on Saturday (November 14).

A battling league draw followed by a thorough dismantling of an inferior FA Cup opponent represents a start - but it would take a league win for the phrase ‘turning point’ to be uttered with any conviction. At present, it can only be whispered.

“I think we play sometimes really well, but we get no points for that,” said Stendel. “You have to score, you have to defend.

“I think it's important that everybody looks for situations that you can score, not only our strikers.

“I think this is important, especially for the next weeks when we have some games against teams around our situation in the table, but also we talk a lot about our defence behaviour, we have to improve that, and I think it was a good step in the right direction today.”

Barnsley: Slicker, Okedina, Gale (Noslin 76), Kay, Larkeche; Johnson (Kelly 63), Connell; Yoganathan, McGeehan (Taylor 71), Cleary (Brownlie 63); Adeyemo (Kirk 45).

Unused substitutes: Ravenhill, Smith, Richards, Alker.

Unused substitutes: Patmore, Matton, Wyllie, Dinanga.

Your Horoscope for the New Moon in Scorpio

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

This lunation is bringing to the light everything that was done in the dark.

New Moons are a time for rejuvenation and manifestation. If you have a lack in your life, then this is the time the collective will work to fill it, whether through a new experience, person, or item. We must make space for beginnings and foolish behavior during new moons. Mind you, we’re all fools when beginning something new, so don’t be scared to get experimental.

For newbies, a New Moon is when the moon conjuncts the sun to the same degree. New moons are for setting intentions and goals to manifest. Take the theme of each new moon (manifestation period) as inspiration to bring forth the best version of yourself! If you find your horoscope helpful or have any questions, please share your horoscope and tag me on social media @monishaholmes.

The New Moon in Scorpio arrives Monday, November 9, 2026, at 2:02 a.m. ET, at 17 degrees Scorpio. I'm actually such a big fan of this new moon, because we're going to see a lot emerging from it—a lot more clarity. Things that were done in the dark are going to be exposed now. But don't expect everything to come out all at once, because new moons are dark for a reason. Look at the sky: it is not bright, it is a dark moon because the Moon sits between Earth and the Sun, meaning the side facing Earth receives no direct sunlight. In astrological terms, that means our emotions are too close to our sense of purpose, that we are not able to recognize how our feelings and ego are influencing life on Earth. We are going to see things unfolding over the next two weeks.This new moon is making a square to Mars, the planet of conflict and drive. A square is an astrological connection that represents friction—think about the phrase “squaring up.” You might be trying to get to the same goal as someone, but you can't exactly agree on how it's going to be done. We may be wanting something that is loud and bold and perhaps even radical. However, this new moon is testing our patience and basically saying “actually, wait.” We need to investigate. We need to understand what's going on before we can make big, dramatic, theatrical changes. We need to do the work of unearthing. Mars is also making a sextile with Venus retrograde in Libra, the last Venus retrograde that we're going to see like this in a while. When Mars sextiles Venus, what these two planets are doing is making a harmonious agreement: Mars is agreeing to expose if Venus is able to give justice, and Venus is willing to get harmony if Mars is willing to put things under a spotlight. This is going to be a very spicy new moon. If you expected boring, please know that boring is not going to be the theme of this week's moon.

Do not expect a downpour of information. We are not going to get a release of what is hidden immediately. Expect for things to take time to adjust, and then eventually information will start to unravel. If situations unfold fast then be careful, accelerated speed may indicate a potential crash out.

Retrogrades are about returns, reflections, re-evaluation. This isn't a time to make any huge, drastic changes or start something completely new. This is a time to look at what was done, how it is affecting us, so that we can begin to plan moving forward. And it's very important that we follow these steps.

Try to zoom out of yourself as the individual and think about what collective or communal justice would be. Perhaps you as an individual felt betrayed or overlooked or disappointed, but chances are, if it's happening in your neighborhood, then it's happening to somebody else on the block. So don't treat things as if they're occurring in isolation. In fact, wisdom comes from learning that very rarely are things happening to just one person.

Make sure that you are not acting out for the sake of acting out. This moon is calling for a sense of decorum, and considering that Pluto is the modern ruler of Scorpio this new moon’s theme is transformation. Currently, Pluto is direct in Aquarius, which means Pluto is governing change happening to the larger community. No change happens in isolation. We may not need huge masses of people to experience a breakthrough, but we do have to be strategic.

Read your Sun sign to gain insight into the version of yourself that you’re maturing into. Read your Rising sign to reflect on your present state. Tested: Electric Genesis GV60 Magma Is Louder Than a Corvette Stingray

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Enable the Virtual Gear Shift mode’s simulated eight-speed DCT and 9000-RPM V-6, and the GV60 Magma howls like a beast.

Welcome to Car and Driver's Testing Hub, where we zoom in on the test numbers. We've been pushing vehicles to their limits since 1956 to provide objective data to bolster our subjective impressions (you can see how we test here).

The Genesis GV60 Magma is, well, red-hot. With up to 641 horsepower under your right foot, the electric crossover is a performance machine. Plus, as we detailed in a recent story, the performance GV60 doesn’t sacrifice any of that performance while in its faux-shifting mode, unlike the mechanically identical Hyundai Ioniq 5 N. In our testing, we found that not only is the GV60 Magma a performance monster, but it shouts like one, too.

See all results for new 2027 Genesis GV60 Magma for sale near 74105

At full throttle and set to its Virtual Shift Mode, which simulates an eight-speed dual-clutch automatic transmission and a 9000-rpm V-6, we recorded the Magma’s interior volume at 102 decibels. That’s considerably louder than the Ioniq 5 N’s 71-dBA max. It’s also louder than a 2027 Chevrolet Corvette Stingray with its 535-hp 6.7-liter V-8. Even when equipped with its optional $5395 quad center-exit exhaust system, the Vette reached a peak of 97 dBA at full chat when we activated its Track mode.

Sure, the Magma’s volume stems from a few lines of code and some speakers masquerading as an engine, while the Corvette has the real thing. But the Magma’s performance is very real, and despite being fake, its simulated shifting and adjustable engine noise are impressive facsimiles.

One Familiar Item Of Clothing Scored Lowest for Intelligence — Can You Guess Which?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

One everyday garment came last for both perceived intelligence and trustworthiness.

One everyday garment came last for both perceived intelligence and trustworthiness.

Track pants, also known as tracksuit bottoms, receive the lower ratings for perceived intelligence compared with blouses, ordinary trousers or long skirts in a study of women’s clothing.

Of the six types of clothing the researchers compared, track pants also received the lowest average ratings for trustworthiness.

Blouses topped the rankings for intelligence and trustworthiness but scored near the bottom for confidence.

Short skirts showed almost the reverse pattern, scoring near the top for confidence and lower than long skirts on both intelligence and trustworthiness.

Short skirts also gave the strongest impression of sexual interest, showing how much people can read into a hemline.

Long skirts fell between short skirts and blouses on confidence, while ordinary trousers scored higher than tracksuit bottoms on both intelligence and trustworthiness.

Onesies gave the strongest impression of happiness and scored about as high as short skirts for confidence.

White clothes suggested a happier wearer than grey clothes did, with black clothes coming last.

These examples show how the impression changes with the question being asked: a blouse came out ahead for intelligence, while a onesie came out ahead for happiness.

The researchers began by asking about 200 people what they thought clothes could tell them about someone and comparing their answers with traits examined in previous research.

They then gathered ratings on 5,000 photographs from online shops, covering five kinds of impressions: intelligence, confidence, trustworthiness, happiness and sexual interest.

Participants formed these impressions from photographs of the garments alone, without seeing anyone wearing them.

The results showed that who was doing the judging explained more of the variation in ratings than which garment they saw.

For example, one person might associate tracksuit bottoms with training for a race, while another might picture someone spending a Sunday on the sofa.

The researchers draw a parallel between judging people by their clothes and judging them by their faces:

“That is, much like inferences from faces, the inferred attributes often lie in the eye of the beholder.”

The study was published in the Journal of Computational Social Science (Rosenbusch et al., 2021).

Psychologist, Jeremy Dean, PhD is the founder and author of PsyBlog. He holds a doctorate in psychology from University College London and two other advanced degrees in psychology. He has been writing about scientific research on PsyBlog since 2004. View all posts by Dr Jeremy Dean

This 80-Year-Old Uses Two Bodyweight Moves to Stay Strong and Independent—No Gym Needed

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Personal trainer Nicola Bretherton shared her mom's home routine, which helps her maintain functional strength, coordination, mobility and balance.

Fat-loss coach Nicola Bretherton helps women build fitness, confidence, and strength for today and for life.

“Especially as you get older...cardiovascular fitness is important but so is building and maintaining muscle,” the founder of the Next Level You program said on Instagram. “[It's necessary] for your strength, bones, and long-term health.”

She highlighted that if changing body composition is your goal, strength training is especially valuable.

“If you’re trying to lose fat and change your body shape, you need to give your muscles a reason to grow and that means challenging them and progressively getting stronger,” she said.

A prime example of maintaining strength and physical capability with age is Nicola’s own mother, whose home exercise routine she recently shared online. The moves build full-body functionality and the ability to carry out everyday tasks.

“This is my mom who will be 80 in October,” she said on Instagram.

“She is a strong lady! She lifts weights at home, does lots of bodyweight exercises—full press-ups! She can even do the splits! I’m so proud of the woman she is—strong, capable, and independent,” she wrote.

“Most 80-year-olds can stand up from a chair, but did you know that most 80 year olds cannot get up from the floor?” Bretherton pointed out. “And that's often because of a lack of strength, balance and mobility.”

The movement also requires coordination and flexibility.

“Most 80 year olds cannot perform a full body weight press up from the floor,” noted Bretherton. “A proper press up requires upper-body pushing strength, core stability and shoulder stability.”

She reminded readers of the real-world transferability of the movement.

“Imagine being able to push yourself back up after a stumble or catching yourself with your hands. This is why you don't train for the body you have now, but the body that you will need later.”

Kate puts together fitness content that covers functional and strength training, cardio, workout challenges, interviews and news. She's often doing gym laundry or listening to music.
