How This Man Transformed His Body with 20-Minute Home Workouts and Minimal Equipment

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

After years of feeling “skinny fat,” Skye Mackintosh set himself a challenge.

Between work, family commitments, and a social life, finding time to exercise can feel like one of the biggest barriers to getting in shape.

Skye Mackintosh set out to prove that you don't need hours in the gym to transform your physique. Instead, he committed to just 20 minutes of exercise every day, documenting his progress over almost a year.

After 340 days, his transformation has attracted thousands of followers. Throughout the challenge, Mackintosh relied on simple home workouts using little more than his bodyweight, a kettlebell, and a handful of basic equipment. More recently, he added a weighted vest to keep challenging himself.

Mackintosh's motivation came after years of feeling unhappy with his physique.

“As a teenager I was naturally skinny,” he wrote on Instagram. ‘Then, as an adult, my age, eating habits, and lack of consistent exercise caught up to me. For 10 years I was ‘skinny fat.’”

Before beginning the challenge, he focused on improving his diet. After cutting out added sugar for a year, he lost 18 pounds. But the weight loss alone wasn't enough to make him feel the way he wanted, prompting him to start strength training at home.

“I cut out added sugar for a year and lost 18lbs,” he shared. “I felt better but not great. I knew that I needed to change and that accountability would help. So I started working out in my bedroom and posting it online. I was humbled and blown away that thousands rallied to support and keep me going. I put on about 8 pounds and made it to day 100.”

Another 100 days later, he'd gained roughly 18 pounds in total. While he felt stronger, he admitted: “I felt strong but didn't feel confident with how I was looking. The extra weight had me feeling a bit sluggish.”

Mackintosh's next goal was to reduce his body fat while holding on to the muscle he'd built. By Day 300, he weighed 3 pounds less than he had on Day 1, but looked considerably leaner and more muscular.

“I feel strong, athletic, and most importantly: happy. I learned to trust myself and the process,” said Mackintosh.

Mackintosh's transformation is a reminder that you don't need marathon gym sessions to make meaningful progress. Twenty minutes of strength training performed consistently can add up over time, particularly when paired with progressive overload and a supportive diet.

As his results show, the best training plan isn't necessarily the most complicated—it's the one you can stick to for the long term.

Kate is a fitness writer for Men’s Health UK where she contributes regular workouts, training tips and nutrition guides. She has a post graduate diploma in Sports Performance Nutrition and before joining Men’s Health she was a nutritionist, fitness writer and personal trainer with over 5k hours coaching on the gym floor. Kate has a keen interest in volunteering for animal shelters and when she isn’t lifting weights in her garden, she can be found walking her rescue dog.

Neuroscientists say people can be "trained" to recognize when they're dreaming — in other words, lucid dreaming may be an acquirable skill.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

During dreams, bizarre scenes you encounter can seem as tangible as reality; you feel as though you're really flying through the sky or being chased through a jungle of bright-purple trees. But in special cases, people realize they're in the midst of a dream as it's unfolding.

Called lucid dreaming, this phenomenon occurs when we suddenly regain our capacity for metacognition, or thinking about our own thoughts, in the middle of a dream. Lucid dreamers can sometimes even control the content or trajectory of their dreams. Now, researchers claim that they've found a way to "train" people to lucid dream by having them meditate and complete virtual-reality-augmented exercises while they're awake.

In a study involving a few dozen participants, the scientists found that repeating this training twice a week for a month significantly increased how often participants experienced lucid dreaming. They say the approach could have practical applications as an intervention for people with nightmares, anxiety or post-traumatic stress disorder — but those applications are theoretical, for now.

If you could learn how to lucid dream, would you? Let us know in the poll below, and add your thoughts in the comments.

Nicoletta Lanese is the health channel editor at Live Science and was previously a news editor and staff writer at the site. She is a recipient of the 2026 AHCJ International Health Study Fellowship, with a project focused on antibiotic stewardship practices in Japan and the U.S. They hold a graduate certificate in science communication from UC Santa Cruz and degrees in neuroscience and dance from the University of Florida. Beyond Live Science, Lanese's work has appeared in The Scientist, Science News, the Mercury News, Mongabay and Stanford Medicine Magazine, among other outlets. Based in NYC, she also remains involved in dance and performs in local choreographers' work.

It should be quite an atmosphere at the Metropolitano as Atletico Madrid take on champions Barcelona, who are two points ahead of them at the top of La Liga.

We expect Barca to edge a closely-fought contest thanks to their extra attacking quality.

Leeds United 1 Tottenham 0: Personality counts as Daniel Farke has everything that Roberto de Zerbi doesn't possess

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Organisation, obduracy. Watching each other’s backs and sticking together.

Strong minds, intensity and feeding off the five-star fuel which is in abundant supply when Leeds attack the Gelderd End, particularly in the second half of matches.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

More especially when those present sense that one of the so called ‘name’ clubs don’t particularly fancy it on a November day in Yorkshire.

It was a win for the home dressing room and the culture that Farke has carefully constructed. He knew it - unity and commitment were the first two words he used to describe his side’s first victory in six games - and his Spurs counterpart Roberto De Zerbi also knew it too.

These are the sort of wins that proper managers love. Ones that are hewn from a bit of adversity. Conversely, they constitute defeats that tell a lot to the vanquished.

De Zerbi said that his side lacked ‘personality’ in the second half and he was spot-on. Personality counts in tight matches.

And while Noah Okafor may have only been on the pitch for just over a minute and a half before his match-winning strike 11 minutes from the end of normal time, nobody could say that the hosts’ breakthrough wasn’t coming or deserved.

Yes, Spurs were entitled to feel hard done by what happened - or didn’t - in the 37th minute with Mathys Tel. Ultimately that wasn’t the point.

A lengthy VAR check scrutinised Jayden Bogle’s challenge on the Spurs man close to the near touchline. The Leeds defender’s left-boot raked the back of Tel’s Achilles, leaving the Frenchman writhing in agony for a short while. Briefly, he did manage to continue before succumbing. Tel left Elland Road in a protective boot and on crutches.

In a post on X, the Premier League Match Centre later confirmed Bogle’s action was viewed as accidental and at low speed. It spared him from a red card, despite seemingly setting a precedent, as similar recent challenges from Jean-Phillipe Mateta and Ryan Sessegnon were deemed sending-off offences.

Spurs submitted a subsequent complaint to Pro Ref following the latest incident. But it was not the reason why they lost. Saturday served as a reminder that when things don’t go in the direction that you like, you show character and crack on.

In the second half, a group of players - several brought in at considerable expense in the summer - lacked cojones, as the Spanish say.

De Zerbi might be Italian, but he wouldn’t need to be told what it meant. He is still finding out about his group as a collective. Farke knows everything about his own and implicitly trusts them.

His Leeds side weren’t bad in the first half, but they weren’t particularly good either. Spurs possessed more of the poise on show. Mateus Fernandes - rightly cleared of a handball offence by an early VAR check - was prominent.

Seeking a fourth successive Premier League win for the first time in over 1,000 days, the visitors laid a decent platform without forging a lead in the opening half.

The second half was a different story. Leeds’ senior professionals in the likes of Dominic Calvert-Lewin and Ethan Ampadu took over.

Their eyes lit up and sensed Spurs uncertainty, while the unforgiving Leeds crowd reminded the visitors of their location south of the Watford Gap. Soft was the polite word employed.

Unlike those in white, the ones in indigo couldn't get going. Leeds, for whom Melvin Bard made an excellent impression after coming on for Gabriel Gudmundsson (hamstring) starred to become incessant in a half when they posted 13 goal attempts.

Nico Elvedi headed a rebound wide after Calvert-Lewin caused consternation. Ao Tanaka spurned a terrific chance, while Antonin Kinsky later made a splendid reaction save to tip over a hooked effort from the recalled Sean Longstaff.

De Zerbi had made a treble change to try and change the narrative. Farke played the telling cards.

Bard's throw-in on the left was flicked on by Calvert-Lewin. Replacement Harry Wilson challenged Rodrigo Bentancur and the ball dropped invitingly for Okafor, who did the rest. On the pitch for just 97 seconds, it was the quickest goal by a top-flight substitute this season.

The number for Spurs to mull over was 51 - the amount of Premier League games in which they have failed to win after going behind.

The late onslaught never came, although James Trafford made one key late save to deny Jan Paul van Hecke. After Bournemouth, that was important.

Leeds United: Trafford; Justin, Elvedi, Muharemovic; Bogle (Rodon 93), Ampadu, Longstaff (Greuv 93), Tanaka (Wilson 77), Gudmundsson (Bard HT); Nmecha (Okafor 77), Calvert-Lewin. Substitutes unused: Zetterer, James, Aaronson, Bijol.

Tottenham Hotspur: Kinsky; Porro (Gallagher 65), Van Hecke, Van der Ven, Robertson; Tonali, Fernandes (Bentacur 65), Gray; Kudus (Williams-Barnett 83), Tel (Solanke 43); Marmoush (Kulusevski 65). Substitutes unused: Gunter, Senesi, Maddison, Davies.

Jordan Thewlis dares to dream after Guiseley striker is the FA Cup hero against Sutton United

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Thewlis' Guiseley had been the better side against a Sutton United team two divisions higher in the pyramid, but had yet to make it count.

When Zac Kennard-Kettle blew his whistle as Jack Tinker tumbled in the penalty area in the 86th minute, the home supporters in a 1,556 crowd roared their excitement. But it soon turned to disappointment when the referee signalled the foul came just outside the 18-yard box.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The free-kick was worked to Thewlis – like Tinker, a substitute – and ballooned into the night sky.

"I think VAR would have given a penalty,” smiled Thewlis. "We probably got... not robbed a little bit, but it was touch and go, wasn't it? You think everything's going against you.

"I think we got booked quite quickly where they might have not. I think the league team does get favoured slightly on occasions."

Thewlis had been denied his moment before. He equalised for Conference North Boston United in a 2019 round two replay against Rochdale, only to miss a huge day out.

"They were in League One," he recalls. "We drew away and then we played them on a Monday at home and lost to a late penalty, 2-1. The draw had been made so we knew we were going, it was Newcastle away.

"That was a bit of a killer, the closest I've come to a third round."

Because four minutes into a minimum of five added to a game already running 21 late because of a medical emergency, Thewlis picked the ball up on the left and bundled his way so close to goal that once the whistle went again, there was no doubt.

"Fair play to the ref for giving the pen because it would have been an easy one to wave away," said Thewlis of Jake Taylor's challenge.

"It was a bit like when we were schoolboys," said the 34-year-old’s manager, PE teacher Ian Richards. "He's won it, he takes it. Cam Wilson's our penalty-taker but Jordan's good from the penalty spot. I was very calm because it was him.

"He's played League Two, National League, National League North. He's made a career on scoring and coming inside like that, weaving through to get a shot off or a penalty."

According to Thewlis: "I just tried to keep my head as clear as possible. The free-kick a few minutes before that, I've sent nine miles in the air. (I was thinking) just don't do that.

"I was confident I'd score. I was confident he'd go the wrong way."

Northern Premier League Premier Division Guiesley’s 1-0 win over fifth-tier Sutton was fully deserved.

"Chances created, shots on target, we were the better team, possession probably evenish, although they might have edged it,” said Thewlis.

Sutton began stronger but their only shot on target was Omar Mussa’s from about 30 yards in the 92nd minute. It was right at Jack Hall.

As the Lions got on top, Elliot Owen missed a good header from a corner and left-back Ollie Brown put an excellent chance laid on by Jack Emmett beyond the far post.

“Usually if you don't take them you can get punished," reflected Thewlis. "But the chances kept coming."

And Richards made good use of a 36-minute half-time.

"I said don't let this affect you," he recalled. "You've got the momentum, try and get that momentum again.

"I talked about how the No 9 (Temi Babalola) would come on to be a bit more direct, which he did. And we picked up where we left off.

"Even though they moved the ball quicker, we looked more threatening. There were a couple of last-ditch tackles by them in the area and a lot of opportunities to score."

The game restarted as more of an arm wrestle. Usually, the last 20 minutes between full-time and part-timers favour the pros. Not here.

"If you get dominated from minute one to 70, 80, you're tired, but we weren't ," argued Thewlis, a property renovator by day.

"We pride ourselves on how hard we work the players in terms of pre-season and we make the training more about quality and high intensity than quantity," said Richards. "And we utilise the squad. I felt a bit of that, a bit of home advantage and a bit of adrenaline got us through."

"There's not many in our league that could put nine on the bench, never mind the quality that we've got," said Thewlis proudly.

Winger Amir Berchil grew in influence as legs tied and in Harrison Hopper had a midfielder to pick him out. He did well to go past Besart Topallaj in the 80th minute, but when the ball came to Kallan Murphy, he slipped. Sent clear in the first added minute, Berchil shot at Brits.

But Thewlis was not going to be denied again.

"I'd rather a winnable one – another National League team," was his answer to the obligatory question of who he would like in Monday's draw. "And at home, it gives us a chance, doesn't it? You start looking at the third round now.

"I'd rather get to that third round. I've never got to the third round."

Neither have Guiseley, beaten twice in the second.

Guiseley: Hall; Odunston, Owen, Mbeka, Brown (Thewlis 78); Hopper, Staniland; Wilson (White 78), Emmett (Tinker 57), Berchil (Iggulden 90+6); Murphy.

Unused substitutes: Roxburgh, Lund, Longbottom, Wadsworth, Sinfield.

Sutton United: Brits; Urpens (Nightingale HT), Kirk, Topalloj, Brookes; Osadebe (Mussa 78), Taylor; Folarin, Knight (Babalola 65), Francis (Ogbonna 65); Carter.

Unused substitutes: Bell, Haigh, Moore-Taylor, Njoku, Amartey.

​Day-night game: The time of year and 36-minute half-time break for a medical incident turned the match into a cricket-style day-nighter with the two halves played in contrasting conditions. But whatever light you looked at it, in, Guiseley were the better team.

Star performers: Ollie Brown missed a golden chance, but was otherwise excellent at left-back and in midfield, Harrison Hopper's passing was tremendous, Amir Berchil's wing-play got better as the gam went on and Jordan Thewlis won it from the bench, but all 15 Guiseley players who took to the field were lions.

Draw: The second round draw is on Monday at 6.45pm on TNT Sports and You Tube.

Gemini North reveals Twin Spirits in Pegasus

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Three high school students in the Project Hōkūlani internship at Gemini North on Maunakea, Hawaii, chose this pair of clashing spirals, cataloged collectively as NGC 7253 or Arp 278, for imaging with the 8.1-meter telescope. It captured this photo — the most detailed ever taken of these galaxies — in October 2025. The students selected this target because it’s in a traditional Hawaiian constellation, Ka Lupe o Kawelo, or the Kite of Kawelo, located in Pegasus. In June 2026, three more interns gave the pair of galaxies a new name: Nā ʻUhane Māhoe Huki Pū i ke Ola, meaning “the Twin Spirits Pulling Together Creating Life” in Hawaiian. The title refers to the way that, over billions of years, the two galaxies will merge, sparking the formation of new stars and planets.

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

American Airlines Updates Vouchers For Stranded Passengers—But Tells Employees There May Be Fewer Hotel Choices

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

American Airlines is updating how stranded passengers get hotel rooms, meal credits and rides during flight disruptions. An internal November memo I reviewed lays out automatic offers for eligible overnight delays and a new way for agents to arrange Uber rides without making passengers download the app. It also warns that employees may see fewer hotels to choose from. Getting passengers out of the customer service line and into a room sooner is great for getting sleep. On a recent American Airlines flight from New York to Austin that was cancelled after a long delay I didn’t even bother getting into the long line at the gate for a room voucher, I just booked something myself nad went on my way. What American Is Changing The update covers American’s Hotel, Meal and Transportation system, which employees call HMT. October 26: standalone meal credits. Agents can issue food and beverage credits for individual reservations affected by qualifying delays, cancellations and missed connections. The system uses flight information to evaluate eligibility and tells agents what qualifies. October 27: automatic offers for eligible overnight delays. The system sends hotel offers and applicable food and beverage credits when its eligibility and automation criteria are met, reducing the need for an employee to initiate the offer. Planned Uber Central rollout. At participating airports, authorized agents would arrange a ride on the customer’s behalf through the same system. The passenger receives a text message or WhatsApp link to start the ride, without downloading Uber. This should now be available but I haven’t seen passenger reports of the broadly available rollout yet (though customers reported it in limited release previously). Hotel inventory changes, date undetermined. American is changing the hotel inventory feed and selection process. Employees are told they may see fewer hotel choices. Automatic Vouchers Already Exist American has been working on this for years. I covered the electronic hotel and meal system in 2019, when agents still had to qualify customers and trigger offers. American was already describing automatic hotel, meal and transportation vouchers in 2025. This spring, they added meal issuance for an entire flight at once. In June, automatic meal vouchers expanded beyond elite members to all AAdvantage members. American’s January app changes already brought disruption assistance together: Their published customer service plan promises, upon request, an approved hotel for an overnight disruption they cause away from your city of residence, transportation there and back, and a food and beverage credit after a qualifying delay of at least three hours. The standard meal voucher I’ve been covering is $12. American recently renamed it a “food/beverage credit” because calling it a meal voucher led passengers to expect it to pay for a meal. How The Uber Change Works American already offers Uber vouchers. The change lets an agent arrange the ride for the passenger. Uber Central supports rides paid for by the business, with trip details sent to the customer. However, American’s instructions still put the hotel shuttle first, followed by an Uber voucher. Uber Central is for exceptions when those options don’t meet the customer’s needs. One passenger reported being at Washington National when agents refused Uber vouchers because a hotel had a shuttle even though it wasn’t running at 1 a.m. Fewer Hotel Choices Isn’t Great The memo’s most concerning sentence is: Frontline teams may see fewer hotel choices displayed than they are accustomed to seeing. Being able to choose a hotel matters. I’ve covered passenger complaints about unsuitable rooms and missing shuttles. Here’s a passenger who said the hotel floor was so dirty they spread sheets over it. If American can’t provide an approved hotel voucher, its customer service plan promises reimbursement of reasonable hotel costs. Its conditions of carriage also say reimbursement isn’t guaranteed when you book directly without written authorization. Credit card trip protections may reimburse required hotel overnights as well. Its conditions of carriage also say reimbursement isn’t guaranteed when you book directly without written authorization. Credit card trip protections may reimburse required hotel overnights as well. American has been working on this for years. I covered the electronic hotel and meal system in 2019, when agents still had to qualify customers and trigger offers. American was already describing automatic hotel, meal and transportation vouchers in 2025.

This spring, they added meal issuance for an entire flight at once. In June, automatic meal vouchers expanded beyond elite members to all AAdvantage members.

American’s January app changes already brought disruption assistance together:

Their published customer service plan promises, upon request, an approved hotel for an overnight disruption they cause away from your city of residence, transportation there and back, and a food and beverage credit after a qualifying delay of at least three hours.

The standard meal voucher I’ve been covering is $12. American recently renamed it a “food/beverage credit” because calling it a meal voucher led passengers to expect it to pay for a meal.

American already offers Uber vouchers. The change lets an agent arrange the ride for the passenger. Uber Central supports rides paid for by the business, with trip details sent to the customer. However, American’s instructions still put the hotel shuttle first, followed by an Uber voucher. Uber Central is for exceptions when those options don’t meet the customer’s needs. Friends, why are any of you still shilling for these pathetic anti-consumer companies on here anymore? It ain’t a ‘free’ market if they can keep doing this and getting away with it. Captured? More like. Cronyism? Mhm. Enough is enough.

$12? For food and beverage? Maybe at a small, regional-flights only airport. At any of the largest, say, 50 in the US? Not a chance. No wonder they won’t call it a meal.

AC gave us $16/person for ‘meals’ after a 4 hour delay on an ex-SFO flight a couple of years ago. You’d need pretty close to double that to cover an average meal at SFO. We had already eaten, as delay was not known until we were boarded. So we received them after we were told we needed to de-board. So what did we do. Took our $64 to a place where we could get snacks. What did we net for a single $16 voucher? A couple of bags of snacks (chips, popcorn, etc). We might have used one for a single bag of snacks and coffee. I suspect this would buy as less today. Hopefully the payout is bigger at airports like SFO, otherwise, AAs premium pitch is going to fall flat (don’t worry, we have more delays than the other majors, but don’t worry, when it happens, we’ll give you a voucher that covers fewer hotels than before, and a ‘food voucher’ that we renamed so you don’t get the impression that it would actually cover a meal – thanks for flying AA’

On a recent trip through Chicago O’Hare International Airport (ORD), I missed my connecting flight to the East Coast. Unless American Airlines (AA) somehow anticipated that my connecting flight would depart more than two hours late, the airline knew well in advance of my arrival at ORD, that I was likely to be stranded at ORD.

To make matters worse, my arrival at ORD was already more than two hours behind schedule because AA had knowingly oversold my original flight and apparently had to beg, borrow, and steal to get approximately 20 passengers off the flight so that it could depart. Given these circumstances, I would have expected AA to proactively make arrangements for displaced passengers, including securing hotel accommodations for those who would inevitably miss their connections.

Instead, when I approached the AA agents at ORD to request a hotel room, they appeared genuinely surprised by my request. Eventually, an agent provided me with a hotel voucher, round-trip taxi vouchers for transportation between the airport and a Courtyard by Marriott approximately 20 minutes away, and a nominal credit for food and beverages.

I find it difficult to believe that the deplorable establishment to which AA sent me was actually operating under the Courtyard by Marriott name. The hotel with which AA apparently has a contractual arrangement to accommodate stranded passengers was filthy, disgusting, and wholly unacceptable. It resembled a seedy, hourly-rate establishment far more than a Marriott-branded hotel. Frankly, it was a place I wouldn’t send my dog. I cannot imagine what AA was thinking when it selected this property to accommodate its passengers.

And the experience didn’t end there. Not a single taxi in the ORD taxi queue would accept AA’s taxi voucher. The same thing happened when I attempted to return to the airport. In other words, even the transportation arrangements AA provided were effectively useless, leaving me to deal with yet another completely unnecessary problem.

The entire experience was appalling. From the oversold originating flight and the resulting missed connection to the unacceptable hotel accommodations and worthless taxi vouchers, AA demonstrated a remarkable disregard for the passengers who depend on it.

I would expect American Airlines to treat a dog better than it treated me. Perhaps, however, in AA’s eyes, paying passengers have been relegated to little more than a nuisance.

That is certainly how I was made to feel.

Graffiti artist tags another GTA 6 ad after Brooklyn mural backlash

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The graffiti artist who tagged Rockstar’s giant GTA 6 mural in Brooklyn has struck again, tagging another GTA ad despite a wave of backlash from fans.

Known as Jelous, the artist first sprayed his tag across the hand-painted mural on Wythe Avenue and North 11th Street in Williamsburg, less than a month after it was completed on October 10.

A black and white “JELOUS” tag covered much of the bottom of the Brooklyn mural, including Lucia’s legs, though Jason, the Rockstar logo and the game’s title were left untouched.

After the backlash, Jelous hid the comments on his Instagram post, but that hasn’t stopped him. He has now tagged another GTA ad [LOCATION/DETAILS], and opened up about why he did it in an interview with Brick and Grit.

A post shared by Brick&Grit (@brickandgrit)

“I grew up playing GTA, so when I saw the ad, I knew I had to put my tag on it. It wasn’t planned. I saw the opportunity and just went for it,” he said.

He also hit back at fans upset by the tag. “People don’t understand graffiti culture. I didn’t go over a mural, I tagged an advertisement. What surprised me most was how personally people took it.”

“It’s ironic how GTA celebrates chaos and lawlessness, yet a little real-life vandalism gets everyone so upset,” he added.

Despite the backlash, Jelous said he was surprised by the attention. “I never imagined it would blow up like this, but it’s cool to see how much conversation a simple tag has started.”

GTA 6 fan emails Rockstar asking for poster and actually gets one

GTA 6 screenshot “leaks” ahead of trailer 2 reveal, but there’s one problem

GTA 6 fans split after spotting hint from supposed Rockstar dev

Comments on his second post are still open, and fans haven’t held back. “Using GTA 6 fame because he knows otherwise his account would be dead,” reads the most-liked comment, while another wrote, “Imagine thinking that those horrible letters look good lol.”

A post shared by jelous.ktd (@jelous.ktd)

Others backed him, though. “Imagine getting mad at a real graff writer doing graffiti on a billboard about a game that centers around violence and living the street life,” one wrote, while another joked, “GTA taught us crime, it’s only right.”

Some even thought Rockstar might be on board, with one fan commenting, “Lol bro GTA might actually like this. I think it’s great promo all around.”

Rockstar hasn’t commented on either tag, or said whether the Brooklyn mural will be repaired ahead of the game’s November 19 release on PS5 and Xbox Series X|S.

Nvidia Board Launches Probe After Reports CEO Was Targeted by Chinese Spy

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Chipmaker's probe comes as the CEO admits to having an affair with the suspected spy.

Nvidia on Sunday said it’s opening an investigation into whether Jensen Huang, the company’s chief executive officer and founder, was targeted by a foreign intelligence effort after Huang admitted having an affair with a business executive who was reportedly a Chinese spy.

The announcement of any investigation follows a series of reports that Huang had developed a “close relationship” with Alice Huang, no relation to Jensen Huang, who is a Chinese citizen and an executive at Megaspeed. Megaspeed is a Singapore-based data center company that buys chips from Nvidia to run data centers that sell computing power to Chinese AI labs.

The New York Times reported in late October that US intelligence agencies had Alice Huang under electronic surveillance for allegedly facilitating the sale of advanced Nvidia chips to China in violation of US law. During that surveillance, officials discovered she was also allegedly funneling information to the Chinese government in Beijing.

The announcement comes as Nvidia has solidified its position as a leader in the AI boom. The cutting-edge computer chips that Nvidia produces are what make AI and AI data centers possible, and that has made Nvidia one of the world’s most valuable companies by market cap.

“Today’s news will not slow Nvidia,” an Nvidia spokesperson said. “Jensen is committed to continuing to lead Nvidia as CEO without interruption.”

A committee of independent directors, working with outside counsel, will oversee the investigation, the board said in a statement. The company added that it “has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board’s inquiry will examine all available information.”

The US has tried to control China’s access to these advanced chips for years. The Biden administration banned Chinese sales of a series of advanced Nvidia chips in 2022, but President Donald Trump relaxed those rules in 2025.  But reports show that there are still illegal operations underway to smuggle banned chips from the US to China.

In his own memo to Nvidia employees, Huang said he was “deeply sorry” for the affair. “I was unfaithful to my wife,” he wrote. “My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did.” Huang, 63, added that he will cooperate fully with the board’s investigation.

The company had previously characterized Jensen Huang’s interactions with Alice Huang as “not unusual or unexpected” given that the CEO “talks with thousands of customer executives every year.”

CNET’s Katelyn Chedraoui contributed to this report.

NAR sees pent-up demand waiting on lower mortgage rates, better sentiment

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

NAR economist Lawrence Yun said pent-up housing demand could be released if mortgage rates ease, inventory improves, and confidence rebounds. NAR data show existing-home sales near 4M, first-time buyers at 22%, and months’ supply at its highest since 2015.  AI Summary

The U.S. housing market is building “significant” pent-up demand that could be released if mortgage rates ease, inventory improves and consumer confidence rebounds, National Association of Realtors’ (NAR) chief economist Lawrence Yun told attendees Friday at NAR’s NXT in New Orleans.

Yun contrasted today’s sales environment with the year 2000, noting that the U.S. population has grown from 281 million to 350 million and payroll jobs from 132 million to 159 million over that period. Housing wealth has ballooned from $7 trillion to $35 trillion, yet existing-home sales are tracking near 4 million this year, well below the 5.2 million homes sold in 2000, he said, citing NAR data.

“Affordability is tough, but people’s incomes are rising, and if somehow mortgage rates go down, think about the potential housing demand that could be released to the marketplace,” Yun said.

On the supply side, Yun said inventory has risen as sales have slowed. Months’ supply is now at its highest level since 2015, and the median home took 33 days to go from listing to contract in September, according to NAR.

“Homes are staying on the market a little longer than before,” Yun said. “With longer days on market, you have to be realistic to the market condition. Otherwise, buyers will reject the homes on the market.”

Yun pointed to consumer psychology as a key missing ingredient. Consumer sentiment has fallen to roughly half its 2000 level and is now worse than during the Great Recession, even as the labor market has added jobs.

“If we can boost confidence levels, maybe that can also change the dynamics,” Yun said.

Jessica Lautz, NAR deputy chief economist and vice president of research, presented findings from the trade group’s newly released 2026 Profile of Home Buyers and Sellers. First-time buyers made up 22% of all buyers, near the series low, and the median age of first-time buyers held at a record-high 40.

Those numbers underscore how younger households are delaying homeownership, even as they form households and enter peak earning years. For lenders and real estate brokerages, that means fewer entry-level transactions and more emphasis on move-up or equity-rich buyers.

“First-time home buyers are struggling,” Lautz said. “They’re not struggling just with housing affordability. They’re also struggling with rent. They’re struggling with student loan debt, and a rising issue for first-time home buyers is childcare costs.”

Lautz noted that sellers reported staying in their homes for a median of 12 years before selling, the longest tenure on record for the survey.

“The golden handcuffs of low interest rate mortgages is true. People are holding on to those properties for longer periods of time,” she said. “It’s why we need to build more housing inventory—especially affordable housing inventory—for the potential first-time home buyers who are out there.”

Despite technology and direct-to-consumer platforms, use of licensed professionals in transactions remained strong, according to NAR’s survey. A reported 90% of buyers purchased through an agent, the highest share in four decades, and a record 92% of sellers used an agent.

“Sellers want agents to price that home competitively, find that qualified buyer, help with marketing, and make sure they get to the closing table in a time frame that they need to do so,” Lautz said.

For real estate agents and brokers, NAR’s data point to a market where transaction volume is constrained more by affordability, rates and sentiment than by underlying demand. A larger population, more jobs and higher housing wealth suggest that activity could accelerate quickly if mortgage rates retreat and confidence improves.

For lenders, a 22% first-time buyer share and a median first-time buyer age of 40 highlight both near-term headwinds and an eventual backlog of delayed demand. Households that are renting longer and juggling student debt and childcare may respond strongly to any easing in rates, pricing or down payment constraints.

For builders and developers, Lautz’s comments reinforce the policy and business case for adding affordable inventory. With existing owners locked in by sub-4% mortgages and staying put for a median 12 years, new construction remains one of the few levers to ease the entry-level shortage.

Yun’s and Lautz’s remarks come as the industry also adjusts to post-settlement commission rule changes and elevated borrowing costs, making accurate pricing, counseling on affordability and clear communication with consumers more central to winning business in 2027 and beyond.

Meet the 27-Year-Old Who’s Trying To Bring Housing Back to Rural Ohio

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

When it comes to the hottest locations that attract young 20-somethings, Mansfield, OH, probably isn't the first place that comes to mind.

For years, Mansfield’s nickname was “The Fun Center of Ohio” because of its family-friendly attractions and its status in the 1970s as a mini-bike manufacturing hub.

More recently, the town of 47,000, located halfway between Columbus and Cleveland, earned the local nickname of “Danger City” after the Westinghouse electric plant left in 1990 and the town fell into deep economic decline.

The 27-year-old serial entrepreneur, who started a painting and landscaping business as a teenager, relocated to Mansfield from Seattle a year ago, hoping to revitalize rural housing through his vertically integrated housing development company, Western Dominion Land.

Vertical integration is a development model in which a single company controls the full life cycle of a housing project.

“In other countries, they’ve figured out that vertically integrating construction means you’re not paying a margin out to every single step," Goehner explains. "If you own the system—from a lumberyard, to producing panels or modular, to buying the land, to developing the land, to building the homes—you're not paying everyone’s markup along the way, and you can actually deliver that home at a reasonable price.”

Goehner is building a factory in Mansfield to produce structured panels that he says will cut single-family home construction from months to weeks and allow him to charge much less than other builders. The first panels are expected to roll off the production line in early 2027.

Currently, it takes 8.8 months to construct a home from start to finish, according to the National Association of Home Builders. Goehner says his process allows a home to be built and move-in-ready in 40 days.

Western Dominion has already built about 500 housing units across Ohio, starting at $200,000 for a single-family home.

Western Dominion focuses on what it calls "attainable housing"—homes priced below what the median household income in the area can afford but not income-limited, meaning they work for the vast majority of families and buyers.

In Mansfield, where the median listing price is $229,000, he says, "single-family homes at $215,000 sold instantly. At $260,000, they sat for months."

As he sees it, 25% of the homes on the market are priced for 60% to 70% of buyers, while the rest of what’s being built is at the top end of the market. Furthermore, only 8% of construction happens in rural areas, despite 20% of the population living there.

"The government is too focused on low-income housing" and low-income housing tax credits, he says. “And the last few housing bills they passed were only structured to deliver essentially more urban multifamily units and incentivize builders to build more low-income housing with tax credits."

In rural communities, he adds, “We have a terrible amount of aged housing inventory. Most of our homes are from before 1970, which would be fine if it didn't cost so much to remodel.

“From the '90s to the 2000s, we’re building a good amount of homes. As a country, we were doing about 1.6 million a year. And then in rural America, we were doing about 220,000 homes a year. And then 2008 hits, and that drops down to about 60,000 homes a year in rural America."

Along the way, smaller builders disappeared, and larger builders were disincentivized from building in rural areas because of the red tape involved.

"If you want to build in rural America and you go to a bank, unless you have all the cash, you have to show comps," he says. "You've got to be, like, ‘All right, we think these homes are going to sell for this much because these guys sold for this much.' And if no one's built for five to 50 years, which is the case in a lot of these communities, that can be difficult."

The added costs of building in rural areas, like having to pull utilities, "mean you're spending $25,000 to $50,000 per lot before you can even start putting a house on it or connecting your utilities to the lot."

Since the COVID-19 pandemic, home prices in rural communities have risen sharply. A Realtor.com® housing analysis of home prices from November 2019 to November 2025 found that median listing prices increased more than 70% in rural counties while in-metro counties saw home prices increase around 30% during the same time period.

"A lot of the politicians we talk to, they go, 'I didn't know that that county needed housing. I didn't know rural needed housing because the population is shrinking.' But then when you look at why the population is shrinking, it's because they haven't gotten any new homes for 20 to 40 years."

"You've got this huge demand. But on paper, on the surface, it looks like there's no demand because the population's declining," he continues. "And that's how you get cities like Holiday City."

Holiday City, near the Ohio border with Indiana and Michigan, has a population of just 48 people, but it is home to three large businesses—Chase Brass & Copper Co., 20/20 Custom Molded Products, and a Menards Distribution Center—which draw around 1,500 commuters from out of state.

"Let's put up, you know, 300 homes, and a portion of the workforce can actually stop commuting an hour or two every day," Goehner says.

Goehner hopes Ohio's new slate of elected officials will help advance rural building by offsetting some of the infrastructure-related costs of building in rural areas. In exchange, he would sell homes at his “attainable” housing rates.

"The government needs to stop spending thousands of dollars a month per unit to subsidize rents when they could simply spend a little bit upfront to make it viable to build at a reasonable price point again," he says.

She was also a senior editor at BuzzFeed, covering lifestyle, fashion, and beauty. She earned a B.A. in women's studies from the University of Pennsylvania and an M.A. in media and cultural studies from the University of Sussex.

Hunters Can’t Really Improve Wild Whitetail Genetics. Here’s Why

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 8, 2026 4:52 PM EST

Get everything from whitetail news and opinion pieces, to advanced hunting tactics and cutting-edge gear reviews.

It was the year we’d been waiting for at the family deer camp. All summer and fall we pulled trail camera photos of more—and bigger—bucks than had ever been seen on the property. It was a far cry from the early 90s, when the bucks my dad and uncles did spot tended to have a signature narrow spread with tall, skinny tines. Now, nearly 30 years later, we’re finally seeing a caliber of deer we weren’t even sure the property was capable of supporting. It’s taken a lot of intensive habitat improvement and discipline, but it’s working.

Still, there was probably a faster way to get here. It should have taken years, not decades, to improve our whitetail habitat. And there are still things we’re trying to figure out—like how to kill those bucks now that we’ve got them. So, I decided to find out what a lot of whitetail hunters get wrong by talking to some hunters who have been succeeding at the management game for a long time.

Typically, a cull buck is one that deer managers have identified as genetically inferior and should be removed from the wild herd. This might be a deer whose antlers are too small or too funky to keep in the gene pool. Studies show that killing these deer solely on the basis of selective harvest (rather than for the traditional reasons of fun, meat, etc.) is ultimately a waste of a good buck tag.

“Many hunters simply believe that you can influence antler quality through genetics in the wild,” says Kip Adams, chief conservation officer of the National Deer Association (formerly QDMA). “We know very clearly that yes, behind a fence—when you can pick which buck is doing the breeding and which doe is doing the breeding—[you] absolutely can influence the genetics. In the wild, it’s very clear we cannot do that.”

This is for a few reasons. For one, does are contributing half the antler DNA to buck fawns. (If you can identify which does have favorable antler genes from your treestand, please share with the rest of the class.) Also, the rut doesn’t lead to quite as many fawns as you might think.

“It’s not like the biggest buck out there dominates all the does and sires the most fawns,” Adams says, noting that the average U.S. fawn recruitment right now is .59, or less than one fawn per doe. “Research shows that most bucks sire very few fawns during the course of their life. We can’t dominate the gene pool that way.”

“We can’t predict what a buck’s son will have—from an antler standpoint—by looking at a buck’s antlers,” says Adams.

Let’s say that again for the hunters in the back: Just because a buck has big antlers doesn’t mean his offspring will. And just because a buck has small antlers doesn’t mean his offspring will have small antlers. (Part of that goes back to the fact that you can’t tell what kind of antler genes a doe is carrying around.)

That all boils down to Adams’ original point: A hunter cannot influence the genetics of a wild deer herd. Selective buck culling to optimize antler size doesn’t work. So, what can hunters do, if they want to shoot bigger bucks? It’s a simple two-part plan rooted in the classic QDM refrain. First, let deer get older.

“We tend to see and hear about more bigger bucks than ever before because older deer are encompassing a higher percentage of the total buck harvest today than ever before,” Adams says. “Hunters today are letting deer get older at unprecedented levels, and that’s the reason that, across the whitetails’ range, we see, hear of, photograph, and shoot bigger deer than ever before.”

The 2021 Deer Report reveals that during the 2019-20 deer season, 39 percent of all antlered bucks harvested were at least 3.5 years old. That’s the highest national average ever recorded.

The second thing you can do if you want to increase antler size? Increase the available nutrition for deer. This means increasing habitat quality, decreasing the number of deer using the habitat (that is, shooting more does), or both.

It’s all good and well to recommend passing bucks to let them mature, but what does that look like on the ground? Some of the most successful big deer hunters follow this advice, and with good results.

Brothers Brent and Justin Cearlock, two of the Illinois hunters behind the world-record whitetail, enjoy considerable success year after year. Part of this is because of where they live, in the prime big-buck county of eastern Illinois. But it’s also because they pass more bucks than they shoot, even with two buck tags apiece per season.

Read Next: Two Years After the World-Record Buck, the Cearlock Brothers Are Still on a Roll

“I have no intention of shooting a deer younger than five, unless it has just a substantially huge rack,” says Justin, who mostly relies on trail camera history to help him establish age estimates. “I don’t care if I think the deer is four. I’m just going to let him get to five, just to see what happens.”

This past season, Brent made the call on a stud buck that didn’t meet this age threshold. Instead of picking up his bow that morning, he grabbed his phone and shot some video.

“I passed a phenomenal deer—the biggest buck I’ve ever let walk in my life,” Brent says. “He was already in the mid-160s, flirting with the 170-inch mark as a 4-year-old deer. I called him Will, because I didn’t know if I would have the willpower to pass him.”

The guys could only imagine what Will might look like in fall 2021. Brent ended up killing a lower-scoring buck that same day: a deer that was at least seven years old, and that he had history with.

“We’re very fortunate to have access to the properties we do. If you don’t have good properties, you’re not going to shoot big deer,” Brent says. “But we do put a lot of effort into it and we pass deer that most people probably wouldn’t pass. There are a lot of guys that say they’ll pass a deer like that. But when they’re standing there and it’s getting dark, they’re like, I’m shooting that deer.”

Brent didn’t regret his decision—the older buck meant a lot to him—but he couldn’t help feeling torn when he learned a neighbor shot Will a few weeks later.

“Passing a big one comes with risk, and I am genuinely happy for the other hunter to get a great buck like that,” Brent says. “But I’d be lying if I said part of me isn’t sincerely disappointed that we just lost what we thought might be our No. 1 target buck next year.”

Often the decision to pass a deer is fraught because of the property itself. Other hunters with permission and neighboring hunters are just two of the many factors that reduce a deer’s odds of surviving into the next season. And the Cearlocks don’t begrudge anyone who wants to shoot younger bucks: They’ve been those guys themselves, earlier in their hunting careers. But the knowledge that a neighbor might shoot a buck isn’t enough to make them abandon their management strategy in the heat of the moment.

“There’s a lot of things that can happen,” Brent acknowledges. “But if you shoot them, they’re definitely not going to make it to five.”

This past deer season, Rick Dahl secured access to a new whitetail property in central Missouri. Dahl believes the new farm, which is in the same county as the parcels he currently hunts, actually holds better potential for bigger bucks. The new property is promising for a few reasons, including its superior soil quality, fewer neighbors to compete with, and the anecdotal evidence that big bucks are being harvested nearby. The fact that the new property doesn’t have any shooter bucks on it yet doesn’t seem to faze him.

“The food supply is very limited on the new farm,” Dahl says, who is a lifelong Missouri deer hunter and chairman of the NDA board. “[But] it’s in the right area and, if we give the deer there what they need to eat, time will tell whether we’ll be able to produce a bigger-antlered deer there.”

Although the property has fertile soil, Dahl says the food supply is limited because it hasn’t been managed for deer yet. A mature forest canopy has prevented understory growth, and many of the tillable fields were used as cattle pasture. Dahl’s no biologist, but he still knows good soil is important for growing big deer.

Dahl conducted a trail camera survey late last year, which revealed a relatively even age-class distribution of deer—and some clues to support his hunch that nutrition is currently a limiting factor for antler growth. The photos of yearling bucks seem to be mostly spikes, instead of fork-horns. And, with the exception of one deer with an extra point, not a single buck on the property seemed to have more than eight points.

“That was really interesting to me,” Dahl says, who noticed four bucks in the 4- and 5-year-old age class that probably scored 140 inches. “It takes a big 8-pointer to score like that. So, there’s clearly an 8-point genetic thing going on there. I will be really interested to see what transpires over the next three to five years as we begin to introduce more food.”

Read Next: 9 Ways to Score Permission on Private Property

What confused me about this particular endeavor—investing time and money in a whole new property that’s going to need careful timber, habitat, and food plot management—is that Dahl currently has a 4.5-year-old minimum age limit agreement with his hunting buddies on the excellent properties they’ve already got. Why not just raise the age limit for shooter bucks to five, like the Cearlock brothers?

“I’m at the point where I think five and a half is the right age [for me],” Dahl says. “But I also realize that everybody’s at a different stage in the maturation process that we all go through as hunters. My hunting partner is a perfect example. He hasn’t shot a lot of bucks. And if we say we’re not going to shoot anything less than five years old, well, what are we doing? We just went from five deer out there we can hunt to two. At some point you’ve restricted yourself. And for me, if there’s only one deer on the farm that I’m willing to shoot, then the question becomes, ‘Is it really fun anymore?’ And maybe that deer jumps the fence and gets shot by the neighbor on October 30. What do you do? Are you just done?”

Happily for Dahl, he doesn’t have to pick. He can tinker with the new farm, and see if those nutritional improvements eventually support the 4.5-year-old bucks he’s interested in.

The Northeast isn’t exactly known for dedicated deer management. This is a combination of multiple factors, including high hunter numbers in states like Pennsylvania and poorer soil quality. But Neil Dougherty, the president of NorthCountry Whitetails consulting services, finds the challenges of creating QDM programs for clients that much more rewarding.

“Iowa is like Disneyland—it’s fake,” Dougherty laughs. “It’s such a wonderful place in those areas where deer have the ability to explode like that, but it’s not the everyman’s hunt. It’s such a foreign thing to most people who are trying to play this deer game.”

When Dougherty examines a deer property, he takes into account 17 different factors—from native habitat to huntability—to determine the property’s potential. He can almost always improve a property to help “unlock the genetic potential” of the deer that are there. But he’s not a magician, either.

“There’s a magic intersection with the amount of food that’s produced on a property, and the amount of deer that are there to consume that food,” Dougherty says, “The problem is, in some big woods country, that could be eight deer per square mile. That kind of hunting sucks.”

While almost all the properties Dougherty works on need some serious rehabilitation of the natural habitat, the biggest problem with a new property usually lies not with the land, but the client.

“When I show up, there’s almost always a hunting strategy that’s flawed,” Dougherty says. “Just about every property has a hunting strategy that does not lend itself to the intersection of the hunter coming in contact with 5-year-old deer. [Those hunters] just haven’t been exposed to 5-year-old deer. They don’t understand what it takes to speak the same language.”

If you broke down those flaws, Dougherty estimates poor understanding of the wind is to blame about 40 percent of the time. That’s a big improvement compared to even 10 years ago. Today’s hunters are generally much more sophisticated at hunting downwind, he says, and being aware of steady or swirling wind patterns.

“Access is the biggest issue I’m finding. It’s super hard to get out of these spots—getting in isn’t that hard to do, but getting out in the dark and not blowing up the area? That requires a lot of discipline. And sometimes we’re limited by the physical characteristics of a property.”

While Dougherty is a habitat expert, his consulting work involves much more than recommending where to put food plots and what to plant in them. At the end of the day, he’s teaching clients how to hunt.

“I would encourage people to not get caught up in this whole genetic thing,” Dougherty advises. “Learn what your [deer] potential is on the property you hunt, know what you’re doing when it comes to habitat, and just be happy with what you’re doing.”

So, even if the stars align and you end up with big deer consistently using your property, the question remains: Can you kill one of them?

As it turns out, I couldn’t last fall. When I did finally get the biggest buck on the place in front of me, I choked. Painful lesson though it was, it was a reminder that managing for mature deer isn’t like shooting fish in a barrel. Sure, it can be a lot easier when you’ve got 5,000 acres and no neighbors or public land for miles around. But that’s unrealistic for most hunters. Even some basic habitat work and personal discipline can improve the number and quality of deer where you hunt.

Natalie Krebs is the Executive Editor of Outdoor Life, where she tackles everything from reporting digital features to producing podcast episodes. Originally from Missouri, she currently lives in northwest Arkansas with her bird dog, Hatchet.

Speedrunners Are Already Breaking The Legend Of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Remake In Absurd Ways

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

NintendoSpeedrunningThe Legend of ZeldaThe Legend of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time

The anticipated remake of the seminal Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time dropped this week. Lifelong fans are returning to a familiar but altered Hyrule. The world originally rendered in 1998 has greater fidelity, hunkier non-playable characters, wiser chickens and, most notably, a jump button. For the regular player itâs a marvel and an adjustment. For speedrunners, itâs a tantalizing playground of tech to be discovered. As the weekend rolls out, and runners dredge the kingdom for secret exploits, theyâre establishing a potential blueprint for record times. As well as uncovering some bizarre horrors along the way.

To say The Legend of Zelda series has some abnormal tech is an understatement. Ever since Link stepped out of his treehouse into a 3D, polygonal world, it was clear the code that binds his reality had a soft underbelly. Ocarinaâs boomerang can rewrite item placement. Wind Wakerâs camera can launch little Toon Link into hyperspeed across the ocean. Skyward Swordâs opening menu seems to exist in some kind of temporal pocket dimension. Nintendoâs fine tuned games have always operated on peculiar physics, but for speedrunners, a return to Ocarina presents an opportunity to rewrite the playbook.

Ladies and gentlemen, we have a speedrun pic.twitter.com/hRN97USavV

The most obvious source for potential run-aiding glitches is Linkâs newfound ability to jump. Like in Breath of the Wild, Link no longer needs to run off a cliff to remember how to use his knees. In standard play, Link can go higher. Speedrunners want to see just how high he can go. Find unassuming geography that can act as shortcuts. Some of the classics, like the building-bounding ‘Hookshot Jump,’ appear to be viable. One of the most popular pieces of tech revolves around open chests. Now that Link can step into them, positioning his feet inside them at just the right angle seems to bristle the game, launching the player like a trampoline. Dropping a bomb in there seems to help as well.

Like previous Zelda games, plenty of glitches lay dormant outside of realtime play. Remake runners are already stress testing start menus and inventory to see what will happen when spammed and agitated. And sure enough, flipping between items in your inventory midair seems to cause Link to float like a leaf in the wind.

“This bug shouldn’t exist lol,” posted ORCinfo. “Once you get used to it, you can jump endlessly in midair. This is gonna break speedruns.”

Defying gravity is obviously useful, but potential sequence breaking exploits is what plummets a runtime from hours into minutes. Stumbling across the proper tech could allow players to skip items, temples, plot beats or reroute them into a more convenient order. This is especially prescient in a game that swaps between two different periods. As runner Zfg1 is chronicling, messing with the hero of time does seem to have a bit of a butterfly effect. While sadly temporary, triggering cut adult Link cutscenes as child Link, and vice versa, momentarily confuses the remake, scrunching his texture files into a neanderthal, or the product of coming out the other end of Amigara Fault.

We can now beat Shadow Temple as child, which automatically turns you into adult. We still have 1 roadblock to get past before this can be used in a speedrun, but it'll be huge assuming we can get past it.

It also looks very funny. pic.twitter.com/s2aoTKCQyY

The day is young as far as how any of this can be applied. Glitches can be awe inspiring, but if they donât contribute to faster runs theyâre just a party trick. Likewise seemingly benign exploits can sometimes pierce through to monumental discoveries, especially as run conditions become more niche. But things are moving fast, even for a scene preoccupied with being fast. The attention to a speedrunning classic means new glitches will be hitting hard and fast. And Link, poor Link, is along for the ride.

Vitalik Buterin Declares Ethereum (ETH) a Store of Value, Not Fee-Burning Token

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum's intrinsic economics is rebuilding itself in motion with tech bugs and the macroeconomic picture overlaying each other in the span of 48 hours. The week wraps up for a major alternative crypto project with a sudden Glamsterdam hard fork stop after testnet launch revealed a dangerous bug in the fee recalculating mechanism, brutally breaking the logic in smart contracts.

As the Ethereum community debated the ability of the network to sustain on old power, Vitalik Buterin showed up with a manifesto which completely zeroes out all the panic around mainnet's decreasing revenue. The "red line" behind Vitalik's updated vision is that the community should stop being fixated on the idea of ETH as "fee-burninng token". For a basic blockchain layer worth $270 billion, attempts to build evaluation on the amount of gas burned are a dead end. For Vitalik, the novel of "ETH as store of value (SoV)" is much more viable amid all the global uncertainty around.

This U-turn, taken by a key visionary of the ecosystem, happened at $2,240 for ETH and may drastically change all the plans developed with a focus on Glamsterdam architecture. The hard fork was initially projected for the logic of blockspace surplus, not the deficit. Ecosystem builders are already readying expansion of L1 capacity with the help of EIP-8037 (tariffs calibration) and EIP-7928 (parallel production) with an aim to raise gas limits up to 200 million units. The logic is that the excess of free space will cheapen the transactions, while token burning rates will gradually be cut.

The market saw a risk of deflation in this concept. Vitalik took a front-step. By his estimates, the historical peaks of burning through EIP-1559 "never were as strong as they seemed". They were dependent on inner cyclical activity of crypto speculations. And it itself was falling once every bear market started.

Attempts to squeeze the premium out of the protocol by any means would push the system toward overly aggressive management and interventions, says Buterin. Ethereum would be required to become a centralized commercial project with inflated costs on research and development in other words. What the market needs meanwhile, amid the rise of AI and regulatory storms, is not a "corporate shitcoin", but what Vitalik calls CROPS - "censorship and capture resistance, open source, privacy and security".

EIP-1559 is not going anywhere, and Vitalik openly says "don't be sad" if the volume of gas burned will forever stay below the 10% mark. The source of value for Ethereum lies in completely another dimension.

Solana's co-founder, Anatoly Yakovenko, unexpectedly aligned with Buterin on this matter. "You can't focus on fees as the products", he said, calling such an idea "absolutely meaningless". According to Yakovenko, the blockchain network must generate real value for the end users, not control the price of getting into the block.

Vitalik is absolutely right that you can’t focus on fees as the product. That’s absolutely meaningless. You need to pick a product, the thing that brings value to people, and do that really well.

There is lots of technical stuff and philosophy around, but what the missed deadline on the testnet and Vitalik's manifesto reveal is that Ethereum opts for long-term, secure, and even a bit boring stability of the basic layer. Infra doesn't try to flirt with the market through deflationary records.

Big-six Brighton? Table since Hurzeler took over illustrates sustained Seagulls success

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Brighton have made a flying start to the season and the table since they appointed Fabian Hurzeler illustrates that their fine form is no flash in the pan.

The Seagulls sit second in the Premier League table, the closest challengers to Arsenal after the first 10 games.

Brighton have scored more goals than anyone else this season, and only the Gunners have conceded fewer.

Hurzeler’s success, though, goes back far beyond the start of the current season.

The German was appointed in the summer of 2024. Then a relative unknown and just 31 years old, Hurzeler replaced Roberto De Zerbi, who had led Brighton to their highest-ever finish – sixth – in 2022-23 but left the Italian left the AmEx after the Seagulls finished 11th in 2023-24.

This is the overall Premier League table since Hurzeler took charge in June 2024…

Under Hurzeler’s management, through two full seasons and the start of the current campaign, Brighton are sixth – equal to their highest-ever position.

The Seagulls have achieved this consistency all the while making a net profit in the transfer market over the last five years.

The only season in which they spent more than they made was Hurzeler’s first, when they invested £191million.

Even that spend, though, could yet yield a hefty profit. According to Transfermarkt, the players bought in that period who are still at the club this term have seen their collective value rise by £69million since their Brighton debuts.

Last season, Brighton made £56million in the transfer market and this summer they turned a profit of almost £11million before leading the pursuit of Arsenal and City who, between them in the recent window, had a net spend of more than £240million.

What NAR’s new MLS policy options mean for data feeds and licensing

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

NAR adopted 4 MLS policy recommendations at NAR NXT 2026, including an optional policy that lets MLSs seek up to 50% copyright interest in certain listing content with opt outs. Additional updates address local rules for data feeds, permitted uses tied to brokerage or valuation services, and MLS Committee qualifications.  AI Summary

The National Association of Realtors (NAR) has approved four multiple listing service (MLS) policy recommendations that increase MLSs’ options to claim copyright in listing content, expand local flexibility on data feeds, clarify permissible uses of MLS data and update qualifications for NAR’s MLS Committee.

The recommendations from NAR’s Multiple Listing Issues and Policies Committee were considered and adopted at NAR NXT 2026 conference on Sunday and are tied to the trade group’s 2026-2028 strategic plan to modernize MLS policies, the association said in an announcement Sunday.

The most consequential change for brokers and MLS operators is a revision to MLS Policy Statement 7.85 and the creation of a new optional policy on copyright protection of MLS data.

Under the revised policy, MLSs may, at their discretion, require up to a 50% copyright interest in certain listing content to strengthen copyright enforcement, subject to quarterly copyright registration requirements. MLSs must give participants the right to opt out of assigning a copyright interest to the MLS, and brokers retain the right to independently use and license their own listing content.

Copyright ownership and enforcement have become more important as listing photos and data are syndicated across portals, IDX sites and marketing platforms, and as MLSs face more frequent questions and disputes about unauthorized scraping and reuse of listing content.

NAR also adopted an optional MLS policy on MLS data feed services. The policy allows MLSs to establish local eligibility and licensing requirements for data feeds, as long as those rules are reasonable, objective and applied equally to all participants.

Even with new local rules, participants must retain access to MLS listing information and data feeds for their own listings.

For brokers and vendors, this could affect how quickly and under what conditions they can obtain broader data feeds for tools such as brokerage websites, back-office systems and analytics products, depending on how individual MLSs choose to implement the optional policy.

A new MLS policy statement reinforces that the use of MLS data must comply with applicable MLS participation or data licensing agreements and must be connected to real estate brokerage services, the preparation of appraisals or other valuations of real property.

NAR said this is intended to address concerns around unauthorized use of MLS content, including use cases that are not connected to providing brokerage or valuation services.

For MLSs, this language provides additional backing for enforcement actions against parties that use listing data for unrelated commercial purposes. For brokers and appraisers, it underscores the need to align technology partnerships and data workflows with the scope of their MLS agreements.

NAR also updated the qualification criteria for service on its MLS Committee. The changes are meant to better reflect today’s MLS environment and ensure the committee includes members with relevant industry expertise and perspectives.

While specific qualification details were not outlined in the announcement, changes to committee composition can influence how future policy recommendations are shaped and how regional and business-model interests are represented at the national level.

“These updates reflect our commitment to ensuring MLS policies remain relevant, effective, and responsive to the needs of today’s real estate industry,” Nykia Wright, the CEO of NAR and a 2026 HousingWire Woman of Influence and Vanguard award winner, said in the announcement. “In line with our 2026–2028 Strategic Plan and significant member feedback, we will continue working collaboratively with MLSs, brokers, members, and other industry partners to support a clear understanding and implementation of these changes.”

Because several of the policies are optional, local MLSs will have to decide whether and how to adopt them. MLS executives and boards will likely review their copyright strategies, data feed agreements and committee engagement structures in light of the new framework.

Brokers, teams and technology vendors should review their MLS participation and data licensing agreements to understand how any local policy changes could affect website data feeds, marketing uses of listing content and third-party integrations.

NASCAR Cup Series Championship Livestream: How to Watch the Race Online for Free

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The 2026 NASCAR season wraps up this Sunday with the Cup Series Championship race at Homestead-Miami Speedway.

Two-time Cup Series Champion Kyle Larson is the favorite to win on Sunday with a 67-point lead over Christopher Bell and a 70-point lead over Denny Hamlin. The Joe Gibbs drivers will need to be perfect if either wants to catch the Hendrick Motorsports’ No. 5 Chevrolet Camaro ZL1 and take home their first Cup Series title.

If you don’t have cable, read on for a quick guide on how to stream the NASCAR Championship race online for free.

The NASCAR Cup Series Championship race will take place on Sunday, Nov. 8 at 3 p.m. ET/noon PT. The race will be available to watch at home on NBC.

If you don’t have cable, you can livestream the NASCAR Cup Series Championship race online using any cable streaming service that carries NBC. Below are three of our favorites — all of which offer free trials that will let you watch the NASCAR race for free online.

NASCAR fans can get a free Cup Series Championship race livestream with DirecTV, which carries NBC in its MyNews and MySports Genre Packs and all of its Streaming Packages. All DirecTV subscriptions start with a five-day free trial. Once the trial ends, packages start at $39.99 for your first two months.

Fubo is another great live TV streaming service that carries NBC for a NASCAR Cup Series Championship race livestream. Packages start at $88.99 a month ($63.99 for the first month), and all of them begin with a five-day free trial.

Fans can also use YouTube TV to stream the NASCAR Cup Series Championship race online. YouTube TV subscriptions begin with a free trial (five to 21 days) and start at a discounted rate of $49.99 a month after that. After the introductory period, pricing settles starting at $71.99 a month.

Phasmophobia players raise over $230,000 for heart charity, more than double last year's total

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The Phasmophobia community has raised more than $230,000 (around £173,000) for the American Heart Association, more than doubling last year's total.

Developer Kinetic Games announced the final figure for this year's Ghost Hunts for Hearts event on 6th November. The charity drive ran from 26th October to 2nd November, and more than 500 streamers signed up to take part. That's the biggest turnout yet for a creator fundraiser run by Heart Liive, the American Heart Association's streaming initiative.

Last year's event raised a little over $100,000.

Congratulations, Ghost Hunters, you've surpassed the community target and raised over $200,000 for our friends at @Heart_Liive!Your kindness and generosity will not go unrewarded, log in to Phasmophobia before the 15th November to claim the Animated AHA Advocate ID Card &… pic.twitter.com/KFQKaqXNCl

Streamers who fundraised could hand out an event-exclusive Advocate ID Card and Badge to their viewers as a Twitch Drop. Kinetic set a target of $200,000 at the start of the week, and once players sailed past it, an animated version of the card and badge set unlocked for all.

"The Phasmophobia community never fails to amaze us, and this year they've outdone themselves," said Tom Dent, lead social media and player experience manager at Kinetic Games. "Thank you to every streamer, viewer and player who took part."

Phasmophobia launched in Early Access in September 2020, and sends teams of up to four players into haunted locations to gather evidence of what's lurking there.

The studio also launched a publishing arm, Kinetic Publishing, earlier this year. Its first game, the starship job simulator Contact Protocol, is due out in spring 2027.

Phasmophobia is available in Early Access on Steam, PS5, PS VR2, and Xbox Series X/S. It's also coming to Nintendo Switch 2, although we still don't quite know when.

Earlier this year, Kinetic Games confirmed Phasmophobia's 1.0 launch had slipped from this year into 2027, as the studio said it was prioritising quality over speed following a rocky reception to its Player Character Update. The studio ended up apologising to players for issues with the long-awaited update, admitting it "missed the mark" and "didn't deliver on our promises to you".
